Kind: bug fix (into `main`); migration no; money yes (invoiced to date and the claim cap on live jobs), stock no, RLS no, payroll no; backup before the production data fix. Size: small to medium (two to three hours). Test on staging: admin@staging.test, J9002 > Cost Centres for the Paid badge (one fake paid claim set by SQL on staging); the import change is proven on the live import page, which is read only until Import is pressed. Simpler: nobody has to notice a ServiceM8 deposit is missing and ask for it; a job comes across with its deposit paid and the right balance to invoice, and the final claim cannot overcharge. Still by hand: the fee line at $0 (above). Best model: Fable 5.1, effort xhigh, because it changes invoiced to date and the claim cap on live jobs with a production data fix in the same go.

## 72. The proposal's savings and plan pages: fit the page, right figures, guesses said plainly, Pylon shown (bug, owner's yes 11 Nov 2026, "build it")

Building in: `claude/simplify-quoting-form-0it08b` (72a first, then 72b, then item 73 on the same branch name).

**72a as built (11 Nov 2026, LIVE, no migration):** points 1, 5 and 6. Proof: `node scripts/proposal-fits.js` fails on the old code (a page 384 px past its sheet, 98 px too wide, 442 px too wide on a phone) and passes on the new; a fresh reviewer swept every page break on 14 quote variants. Calls made in the build:
- Find plans keeps a flat and a time-of-use plan of the same name (a meter allows one or the other); only a dearer plan of the same name and tariff is left out.
- The savings sheets lost their corner graphic, so figures never sit on it.
- Not checked: whether Pylon's API gives the proposal link (app.getpylon.com is blocked from cloud sessions). The link is still pasted on step 3.
- Found while reading for 72b, not fixed here: the client's proposal is only given five of the savings inputs (`get_quote_by_token` passes `solar_price`, `battery_price`, `current`, `alt`, `plan_options`), so night charging from the grid, selling from the battery and the bill's GST setting typed on the bill card never reach the proposal. No quote uses them today. Fixing it, and passing the new assumptions, needs that function redefined: one migration in 72b, SQL to the owner first.
- Left as they are (same on the old code, outside this change): the Your system table and a variation's price table do not carry over past one sheet (26 equipment rows or 34 variation lines), the investment breakdown stops after 12 sheets, and a quick estimate's rates table is 30 px too wide on a phone.

**72b part 1 as built (11 Nov 2026, LIVE, no migration):** points 2, 3, 4 and 7 (the figures, the staff warnings, the plain page, the export limit typed by hand). Two review rounds on the maths (energy balance over 6,000 random stages, the plan table against the savings page in 3,200 cases). Q1014 now reads: saving $8,199 a year (was $8,397), 10.6 years (10.1), $80,170 over 10 years and $158,516 over 25 at today's prices ($217,760 if prices rise 3% a year; was $286,266), 10,499 kWh a year lost to the 5 kW limit, Red Energy $265 a year less than the current plan (was $380). Sent quotes Q1005 and Q1008: the yearly saving and payback are unchanged ($20,929 and 2.4 years; $12,452 and 2.5 years); their 10 and 25 year figures are lower (today's prices, battery counted 15 years) and Q1005 no longer shows a payback each for the solar and the battery. Calls made in the build:
- "Is the existing system being removed" is its own answer on step 1 ("Existing system after this job"), saved under `existing_system`, a key the proposal is already given, so no migration was needed for it.
- The plan options rows are all moved by the amount the real bill sits above or below its rates, so the difference between two rows is the difference between the plans.
- A battery's saving is counted for 15 years (the owner can change the normal value; per quote it comes with part 2).
- `quote.html`'s weight budget went up 5 KB (805 to 810 KB): the maths now says what it assumes. The staff-only checks moved out to `solar-roi-staff.js` first, which took 17 KB off the client's page.
- Part 2 (the Savings assumptions page, discounts and credits off the bill, and the keys the client's page is given) followed with migration 20261130080000: see the next note.

**72b part 2 as built (11 Nov 2026, owner: "Yes build", migration 20261130080000):** point 8, plus the two leftovers from part 1. Calls made in the build:
- The Savings assumptions button sits on the bill card (step 1), next to the preview it changes; step 5 lists the blocks and points there. It is one panel, not a new page, so `project.html` took a button and two lines.
- Five blocks can be shown anyway: no bill read, no kWh, no rate, no bill total, existing solar size not known. No system size and no price are not overridable: the fix is one number on the quote itself and the panel says so.
- No bill read needs use (kWh a day) and a rate; supply charge and feed-in are optional. The proposal then prints "We did not have your bill" with the typed figures, and nothing is labelled "from your bill".
- A typed value outside its limits is refused with the limits in the message; a blank box is the normal value; "Back to the normal values" clears every box.
- Discounts and credits: the bill reader takes a percent off usage, a percent off the bill, fixed credits and other charges as their own figures, so a bill with a pay-on-time discount no longer shows as a gap between the bill and its rates. Bills read before this keep the gap line until read again.
- `get_quote_by_token` passes the four bill figures and `export`, `grid_charge`, `bill_gst`, `assumptions`, `overrides` from `roi_inputs`. The function is otherwise word for word migration 20261128090000.
- `quote.html` stayed inside its weight budget: the size suggestion moved to `public/js/solar-sizing.js` and the staff wording of each gap to `solar-roi-staff.js`.
- Who can open it: the pricing roles (the bill card's own rule). Narrowing it to Admin and Finance is a one line change if the owner wants it.
- Fixed in the same change, from a second review of the live figures (part 1): on a plan with a midday band (solar soak, free power at lunch) a home with solar could end up buying up to 25% more or less than its bill said (worst case $68 a year on a battery saving): the total is now brought back to the bill (within 1% on 1,200 made-up homes) and the page says "our closest fit" when the bands do not line up; a page where the bill goes up no longer lists savings over 10 and 25 years or draws Savings add up, and the staff get a warning that the proposal shows the bill going up; a typed export limit over 1,000 is taken as watts and not applied (the staff are told); clearing a typed box on step 1 now clears the answer; an older answer only moves to "earlier answers" for a question that was once another question; a plan option in credit reads "in credit".
- Fixed from the review of part 2 before it shipped: the client's page is given an override's switch and figures only (the first cut of the SQL would have passed the staff's reason and name); an override stops acting once its block is gone (a rate typed later, a bill read later); figures typed in place of a bill never print as "from your bill" and are not lifted 10% for a company client; a rebate or concession on the bill follows the customer to every plan in the plan options (identical rates read "about the same" again); an existing solar size typed for an override keeps the words already there ("to be removed" still counts); the proposal asks for the whole bill when there is none, not for its total page.
- Not changed: a system that replaces a bigger one, with a battery, can show a 25 year saving lower than its 10 year saving (after the battery's 15 years only the solar's share counts, and that share is a cost). The staff warning for a bill that goes up does not fire there because the total still saves.

**72c as built (11 Nov 2026, LIVE, no migration; owner: "Peak sun hours should already account for cloudy days as it is averaged over the year, so putting both in might be making the figures worse", then "Yes" to the plan).** Checked on Q1014 and two made-up homes:
- The sun was never taken off twice: the clear and overcast days are scaled so the year still adds up to kW x the sun figure x 365 (Q1014's new panels make 42,761 kWh a year with the split and without it). The split does lower the saving, because the same sun arrives in lumps (a clear day makes more than the home and battery can take, an overcast day does not fill the battery): against every day being the same, 3% lower for 6.6 kW of solar alone, 9% for 10 kW with a 13.5 kWh battery, 11% on Q1014.
- Built: the proposal's **Sun** line now says the figure is the whole year's average with cloudy days and normal losses already counted, and **Clear and dull days** is its own line that says it does not change the yearly total. The Savings assumptions boxes say the same, and that the sun box is what a kW of panels makes, not peak sun hours.
- Built: the normal sun figure follows the site. 3.9 kWh per kW a day is the Clean Energy Council's Sydney figure and was used for every site in STC zone 3; its Brisbane figure is 4.2. A zone 3 site with a Queensland postcode (4000 to 4999) or a Northern Rivers one (2460 to 2490) now takes 4.2 (`roiSiteYield`, from the quote's site address; no postcode keeps 3.9). The size suggestion uses the same figure.
- What moved: Q1014 (draft) $8,199 to $8,329 a year. The two sent quotes' live links: Q1008 $12,452 to $12,833 (2.5 to 2.4 years), Q1005 $20,929 to $21,596 (2.4 to 2.3 years). PDFs already emailed keep the old figures.
- **Still open, the owner's call:** the normal "7 clear days in 10, an overcast day making about a third" is an assumption that has not been checked against weather records (the weather data site is blocked from cloud sessions). 8 in 10 would lift Q1014 to $8,794. It can be changed per quote on the Savings assumptions page today; the normal value is `ROI_ASSUMPTIONS.dayTypes`.
- Also found, not built: Pylon's data already carries the proposal link (`pylon_data.summary.web_proposal_url`), so the Pylon design link on step 3 could fill by itself.

Owner's ask (11 Nov 2026): "The quote doc needs fixing. The calc page runs off the page. So does the plan options. Only Red Energy came up as an option and there was heaps of Red Energy plans, nothing else. The Your savings page needs to be looked over and made sure it's correct. Be upfront about the guesses in the calcs. Make it super simple for the owner to read and understand." Then: "Pylon isn't showing in the solar proposal too."

Looked at on Q1014 (31.04 kW with a battery, Energex, 4211), rendered in a local browser from its real figures, and the maths re-run in node by a second reviewer.

**Layout (cause found):**
- The savings table cannot split across sheets, so it moves whole to the next sheet, leaves the first half empty, and its last rows sit on the corner graphic; a longer one runs off the bottom.
- The plan options table is 98 px wider than an A4 sheet and 442 px wider than a phone screen (four long headings that cannot wrap).
- On a phone the three charts sit side by side at a third of the screen each and cannot be read.

**Plan finder (cause found):** the list is the 10 cheapest plans. For that home 23 retailers have plans; Red Energy has 112 near-identical ones and the highest feed-in (5 c), so all 10 were Red Energy.

**Figures (the sums are right; the inputs and the basis are not).** Q1014's page says: bill now $9,533, after $1,136, saving $8,397 a year, pays for itself in 10.1 years (solar 7.8, battery 13.7), $286,266 over 25 years.
1. The export limit is never applied. The page says 32,682 kWh a year go to the grid; a 5 kW limit lets out about 13,700. Saving $470 a year too high ($111 if the limit is per phase).
2. The battery is 90 kWh in the maths (typed, and 10 x 9 kWh in the lines) while Pylon and the stage text say 6 x SigenStor BAT 10.0 (about 56 kWh usable). Nothing warns staff and the proposal shows both. The home uses 39 to 45 kWh at night, so the maths gets nothing from the kWh past about 56.
3. The existing 1 kW is being removed, but it is still counted as generating after the install ("on top of your existing 1 kW"). $86 a year too high.
4. A home that already has solar: the bill's kWh is what was bought, not what was used, and the maths treats it as the whole use. About $287 a year here.
5. "Now" is the bill total, which on this bill includes $419 a year of discount or credit, while the saving is priced at full rates. The page calls it "at today's rates".
6. The plan page works its "with the system" bill a second way: $1,102 beside the savings page's $1,136 for the same home.
7. Solar and battery paybacks come from a default price split that leaves both inverters with the battery, so the solar looks quicker (7.8 years) and the battery slower (13.7) than a fair split (about 9.8 and 10.7).
8. "Pays for itself in 10.1 years, paid off in year 11" is worked at today's prices, while the chart's line rises 3% a year and crosses at 9.2 years.
9. The 25 year figure assumes the battery and inverters last 25 years with no replacement and that feed-in rises 3% a year: about $54,650 of the $286,266 rests on that.
10. The battery's charge level is only run for two days, not until it settles ($35 a year).
With 1, 3 and 4 fixed Q1014 reads: saving $8,178 a year, 10.4 years, $278,800 over 25 years.

**Guesses:** the maths makes over 20 (when the home uses power through the day, 7 clear days in 10, the bill's quarter standing for the year, battery life, the price split and more). The customer is told five, in small grey print at the very end, after the plan disclaimer.

**Pylon:** item 70 did not change it (the Pylon card is still on step 3 of Solar and Battery quotes). On the proposal Pylon appears as one line of equipment on The project page and the Your system table, and only after Pull from Pylon. The "View interactive design" button needs a pasted Pylon link, and sits on the Scope of works page, which does not print when the quote has no scope text. Of the 9 Solar and Battery quotes, 1 has Pylon data (Q1014, no link), 2 have a link and no data, 6 have neither.

Plan (bug fix into `main`, no migration):
1. **Fits every sheet and a phone.** The savings table splits across sheets with its headings. Plan options become a short list: plan, yearly bill, difference, with the plan ID and what to check on a line under each. Charts stack on a phone. A kept proof step renders a proposal and fails when any page is wider or taller than its sheet.
2. **Right figures** (`solar-roi.js`): the export limit is applied hour by hour and a "Lost to the export limit" row shows when it bites; one new yes or no on step 1, "Is the existing system being removed?", shown only when there is one (only the salesperson knows this); a bill with existing solar counts what the old system supplied; discounts and credits are read off the bill as their own figure and used on both sides; one basis for now, after and the plan page; one payback for the whole system unless the two prices are typed (no default split); the payback and the chart on the same basis (today's prices), with the price rise shown as a second figure; the battery's saving counted for 15 years in the 25 year figure; the battery run until its charge settles.
3. **Staff warnings on the bill card and step 5:** the battery kWh differs from the Pylon design or is more than the home uses at night; the export limit is biting; the bill is from a home with solar.
4. **Simple, and upfront about the guesses** (the reader is the home owner). Top of the page, three figures in words: your bill now, your bill with this system, what you save a year, then "pays for itself in about N years" and a likely range (the same maths run with less sun and with more daytime use). Then the charts. Then two short lists side by side: **From your bill** (days, kWh, rates, feed-in) and **What we assumed** (when you use power, sunny days, battery efficiency and life, export limit, prices), each with its value, and one line on what would change it (an EV, a pool, a new plan). The working table goes on its own sheet, "How we worked it out". Estimates in whole dollars, with "about".
5. **Plan finder:** one row per retailer (its cheapest plan for this home), up to 10 retailers, "more from this retailer" opens the rest.
6. **Pylon on the proposal:** the design box (equipment line and the View interactive design button) prints on the Your system page whenever the quote has a Pylon link or Pylon data. Step 5 says "No Pylon link: the proposal has no design button" on a Solar or Battery quote. At build, check whether Pylon's API gives the proposal link, so Pull from Pylon fills it by itself (the cloud session cannot reach app.getpylon.com today).
7. **Export limit typed by hand (owner, 11 Nov 2026).** Step 1's Export limit becomes a number in kW, in total across the phases, with one tap for No limit, Zero export, 5 and 10. Blank means no limit is applied and the proposal says so under What we assumed. Answers already saved ("5 kW", "Zero export"...) read as they are. A Battery quote gets the same box on the page in point 8.
8. **Savings assumptions page (owner, 11 Nov 2026: "a page we can click on and override the hard blocks, and edit any of the assumed values if we can't change them elsewhere").** A link on the bill card and on step 5 opens one page for this quote, in two parts:
   - **Every assumed value**, with its normal value, the value in use and a box to change it: export limit; sun (kWh per kW of panels a day); clear days in 10 and what an overcast day makes; when the home uses power (evening heavy, even, daytime heavy, or a typed share in daylight); the season factors; kWh a year when the bill's quarter is not typical; what an existing system supplies direct; battery efficiency, charge rate and the years its saving is counted; price rise a year; panel output loss a year; grid emissions. Values that already have a home (the bill's figures, the rates, the prices, the battery size) are linked, not repeated. A changed value shows what it was changed from, who and when, with Reset.
   - **Every hard block that is on** (today seven: no bill read, no kWh on the bill, no rate, no bill total, existing solar size unknown, no system size, no price). Each has "Show the figures anyway", the value to use in its place and a short reason. The figures then print.
   - The proposal's What we assumed list always prints the value in use, and an overridden block prints in plain words ("We did not have your bill: 22 kWh a day is our estimate"), so the client sees what the figures rest on. Each change and override goes in the quote's activity with the name and reason.
   - Who: Sales, Finance and Admin (the owner can narrow it to Admin and Finance, as the send check is). The send check's own override stays where it is (Email quote, Send anyway, with a reason).
   - Saved on the quote (`projects.roi_inputs.assumptions` and `.overrides`, no migration); the code is its own file, `public/js/quote-savings-assumptions.js`.

Build order, two releases so the visible faults go first: **72a** points 1, 5 and 6 (fits the page, plan finder, Pylon box: half a day), then **72b** points 2, 3, 4, 7 and 8 (the figures, the plain page, the export limit and the assumptions page: a day and a half).

For the owner to answer (the build goes ahead on the default): Q1014's battery, 10 x 9 kWh (90) or the 6 x BAT 10.0 in Pylon (default: nothing changed, the new warning shows); "5 kW" export limit on a 3 phase site, in total or per phase (default: as typed, in total); battery life in the 25 year figure (default 15 years); what you expected to see from Pylon (default: the design button and equipment, as in point 6).

Sent quotes: the live links of Q1005 and Q1008 (sent, with a bill read) will show the corrected figures; the PDFs already emailed keep the old ones. Their before and after go in the PR.

Done means (staging, sales@staging.test, a Solar quote with a bill read, a battery and an export limit): (1) Make PDF: no page runs off its sheet, the savings table carries over with its headings, plan options fit. (2) The link on a phone: charts one under the other, nothing scrolls sideways. (3) Step 1 > Find plans: 10 different retailers, one row each. (4) Type 7.5 in Export limit: "Sent to the grid" drops and "Lost to the export limit" appears; set the existing system to removed: "on top of your existing" goes. (5) The savings page opens with three figures and a range, lists From your bill and What we assumed, and the plan page's "with the system" bill is the same figure. (6) Bill card > Savings assumptions: change the sun figure from 3.9 to 3.6 and the proposal's What we assumed shows 3.6; on a quote with no bill, tick Show the figures anyway, type 22 kWh a day and a reason, and the figures print with "We did not have your bill". (7) A quote with a Pylon link and no scope text shows the design button on Your system.

Touches: `public/js/solar-roi.js` (the maths, checks, page wording, assumptions read from the quote), `public/quote.html` (savings, plan options, Your system, page splitting), `public/project.html` (bill card: plan list, warnings, working, the link to the new page), `public/js/quote-savings-assumptions.js` (new: the assumptions and overrides page), `public/js/template-questions.js` (export limit as a number, one question), `netlify/functions/read-electricity-bill.js` (discounts and credits), `scripts/steps/keep/proposal-fits.js` (new), `?v=` bumps. Overlaps: item 73 (same `quote.html`: build 72 first, then 73, in this chat). Kind: bug fix (into `main`); migration no; money, stock, RLS or payroll no (no invoice or price changes; it changes the savings figures a client is shown). Size: large (two days: 72a half a day, 72b a day and a half). Test on staging: the Done means above; the AI key for Read bill. Cloud sessions cannot open the staging site, so the proof is a local browser run on Q1014's real figures unless `*.netlify.app` is allowed. Simpler: the page says what it rests on, so nobody has to explain it; the plan list shows every retailer without scrolling past one; staff are warned about a wrong battery size or export limit before it goes out; nothing about the estimate is locked, every assumed value and every block can be changed on one page, and the client is told what was changed. Still manual: one yes or no about the existing system (only the salesperson knows); the assumptions page is there to be opened only when the normal values are wrong for a job. Best model: Fable 5.2, effort xhigh, because it changes the savings figures clients decide on.

**72d. Cloudy days start with what the sunny days left (owner, 12 Nov 2026, LIVE straight to `main` the same day, no migration).** Until now the estimate ran each kind of day on its own until the battery settled, so an overcast day was costed as if it followed another overcast day with the battery already flat: on Q1017 (25 kWh battery, 13 kW existing solar, a 3c feed-in) that left 1,137 kWh a year still bought and a bill after of $1,281, and a 40 kWh battery gave the same figure. The days of each season now run in sequence (7 clear then 3 overcast by default, the quote's own share when typed) with the battery's charge carried from one day into the next, and the whole sequence repeats until it settles. Q1017 moves to 813 kWh still bought, $1,177 after, payback 9.9 years instead of 10.6; the 13.5 kWh figure moves by $1, since a small battery is empty by morning either way. A home with no battery is unchanged. What still holds the bill up on Q1017 is the supply charge ($2.67 a day, $974 a year) and the winter top-ups; the bill shows 15.7 kWh a day bought with 13 kW on the roof, so the home uses about 29 kWh a day in all, not 13. Touches: `public/js/solar-roi.js` (`roiRunStage`), `?v=` on `project.html` and `quote.html`.

**72e. The working goes after the payment schedule (owner, 12 Nov 2026, LIVE straight to `main` the same day, no migration).** On the proposal, "What we assumed", the line on what would change the figures and the yearly table moved off the Your savings page onto their own "How we worked it out" page after the investment page (and its breakdown, when shown) and the payment schedule, before the photos and terms. The savings page keeps the three figures, the pictures, From your bill, anything still needed from the client and the other plans, with one line saying the working follows the payment schedule. A proposal without the three figures keeps everything on the savings page, since the table is its only figures. Q1017 went from savings over four sheets to two. Touches: `public/quote.html`, `public/js/solar-roi.js` (`roiListsHtml` takes one list), `scripts/page-budgets.json` (quote page 814 to 819 KB: the page was 2 bytes under its budget, and the new page section cannot fit without trimming unrelated code).

## 73. Proposal pictures: a title picture per proposal type, an About picture, pictures on Your system, AI photo tags and search (owner's yes 11 Nov 2026, "build it")

Building in: `claude/simplify-quoting-form-0it08b`, after item 72.

Owner's ask (11 Nov 2026): the proposal's front page is stuck on the shed roof and does not change with Settings; one title picture per proposal type; one picture for the About Thomson Energy block (same on every proposal); pictures added to the quote for the Your system page, from the computer or the phone, such as their wall from the site visit with the inverter we intend to install cropped in; AI tags on every picture uploaded to a job, so searching Sigenergy in the quote or the search bar finds them, and a quote with Sigenergy on it offers recent installs to add, captioned "Similar system" or "Indicative picture".

What is there today (read 10 Nov 2026):
- The title page picture is fixed in the code (`quote.html`: `/img/showcase/shed-solar.jpg`, or `battery.jpg` on a Battery proposal). Settings > Cover page photo never reached the proposal, which is why changing it did nothing; that box was removed by item 70 the same day. The picture uploaded there is still saved (`company_settings.cover_photo_url`).
- The About block's picture is one of six built-in showcase pictures, picked by the quote's link.
- Your system is a table of the Pylon equipment with no pictures. Step 4's "What it will look like" pictures print on a Photos page at the back, with no caption.
- Job pictures (`project_photos`: 1,027 rows on 46 jobs, 223 in the last 30 days) have a caption and nothing to search by. The search bar does not look at pictures.

Plan, in three builds on one branch (they touch the same pages), each whole on its own:

**73a. Pictures the proposal uses (a morning).**
1. Settings > Company Details > **Proposal pictures**: one Title page picture for each proposal type (New Build, Solar, Battery, Quick Estimate, Renovation, Service Work) and one **About Thomson Energy** picture. A type with no picture keeps the built-in one. The picture already uploaded as the old Cover page photo starts as the title picture for every type except Battery, so the front page changes the day this ships.
2. Step 4 of the quote: "What it will look like" becomes **Pictures of your system**. Add from the computer or the phone (camera or gallery), or pick from this job's own pictures. Each picture has a caption with one tap for "Your site", "Similar system" or "Indicative picture", or your own words. They print on the **Your system** page with the caption under each, so a client always knows when a picture is not their own house. The page prints whenever there is a picture or Pylon equipment. Pictures already on a quote carry over as they are.

**73b. AI tags and search (a day).**
3. Every picture added to a job is read by AI in the background, by itself: the brands it can see (Sigenergy, Fronius, Sungrow, Victron, WINAICO...), what the thing is (inverter, battery, panels, switchboard, meter box, roof, cabling), the model when the label is readable, and what kind of picture it is (finished install, before, in progress, damage, label, paperwork). The job's own equipment list is given to the AI as a hint, and a brand is only tagged when that equipment is in the picture. It also marks a picture **fine for proposals** when it is a finished install with no people, faces, number plates, street numbers or paperwork in it.
4. Pictures already in the app (1,027) are tagged once. The first 50 run as a dry run that only lists the tags, for the owner to look over with the cost per picture, before anything is saved (new agents start read only).
5. Search: the top search bar gains a **Pictures** group (type Sigenergy: thumbnails with the J number, opening the job's Site Photos on that tag), and the Site Photos tab gets a search box. A tag can be added or taken off a picture by hand when the AI has it wrong.
6. In the quote, step 4 shows **Recent installs with this gear** by itself: pictures tagged with the brands on this quote (from the Pylon equipment and the stage lines), fine for proposals, newest first. Tick one and it goes on the Your system page captioned "Similar system".

**73c. Put the equipment on their wall (a day).**
7. A picture editor on step 4: open the site picture (their wall), place a second picture on top (the inverter or battery), drag it, size it, crop either one, save. The result is a new picture on the quote captioned "Indicative picture" by itself. Works with a finger on the phone and a mouse on the laptop.
8. Settings > **Product pictures**: a clear-background picture per model (from the maker's media kit), uploaded once; the editor offers the ones matching the quote's equipment first. An ordinary photo can still be placed, cropped to a rectangle.
9. **Owner's question, 11 Nov 2026: can the AI find a similar installed battery or inverter and swap the background, so it shows as installed on this customer's wall?** Finding it: yes, that is the tags in 73b (the editor offers recent install pictures of the same model). Swapping the background: not by the AI TEops uses (Claude reads pictures, it cannot make or change one). Two ways to get there, offered for the owner to pick:
   - **A. Cut out and place (recommended, in this build).** One tap, "Cut out the equipment", runs a background removal tool on the install picture; the cut-out lands on the wall picture to be dragged and sized. Real equipment on their real wall; the light and shadow will not match perfectly. The tool is either one that runs in the browser or an outside service with its own key and a small cost per picture: which one is settled at build, with the cost, before it is switched on.
   - **B. A picture-making AI from another provider** is given both pictures and draws the equipment as installed. It looks the most real, but it redraws the whole picture: it can change the wall, invent conduit, or get the model's look and size wrong; the client's home pictures go to a second AI provider; and it needs that provider's account, key and cost. If wanted: a trial on five pictures first, off by default, every result captioned "Indicative picture, drawn by AI".
   Either way the caption says it is indicative, by itself.
   **Owner, 11 Nov 2026: way A.** The equipment is cut out automatically and we place it ourselves. Also cutting out by hand, three tools in the same editor: **Trace** (draw around the equipment with a finger or the mouse and everything outside the line goes), **Box** (a plain rectangle crop), and **Rub out / Bring back** (a brush to tidy the edge after an automatic or traced cut, or to bring back a piece it took by mistake). Undo steps back one cut at a time, and the original picture is never changed. Way B is not built.

**73a as built (11 Nov 2026, LIVE 12 Nov 2026, migration 20261130090000):** points 1 and 2. Calls made in the build:
- The Settings card saves each picture the moment it is chosen (no Save button to forget), and "Use the built-in picture" takes one off.
- Step 4 has three ways in, and each sets the caption by itself: **Add pictures of their site** (captioned "Your site"), **Add other pictures** ("Indicative picture", one tap to "Similar system") and **From this job's pictures** (the job's Site Photos, "Your site"). A picture with no caption shows a line saying the client may take it for their own place. The box saves itself; Save scope of works no longer carries the pictures.
- A Solar or Battery proposal prints them on Your system, two across, the whole picture (never cropped), caption under each. The other proposal types have no Your system page, so theirs print where they did, on the Photos page under What it will look like, now with the captions.
- The Your system equipment table now carries over onto the next sheet (it ran off at 26 rows).
- **From this job's pictures** offers the job's Site Photos and the site visit's checklist pictures. A picked picture is copied into the proposal's own folder, so deleting the original later never empties a proposal a client is reading.
- The PDF is remade once, 4 seconds after the last picture change, not on every tap. A caption saves when its box is left; leaving the page mid-save asks first.
- Settings previews are cut the way the proposal cuts the picture: upright for the front page (it fills a whole sheet, so a tall picture suits), wide for About.
- A fresh reviewer's 8 findings were fixed before the merge (the two above, an unescaped About picture link on the client's page, two boxes saved together losing one, and smaller ones).
- Each migration ships with its build: this one holds the two settings and the public settings function; the picture tags (73b) and product pictures (73c) bring their own, so 73a could go to staging whole.
- `quote.html`'s weight budget went up 2 KB (810 to 812 KB) for the pictures and captions, and 2 KB more on `staging` (to 814 KB) when item 72c's wording from `main` met them. 484 KB of that page is the whole staff library (`supabase-client.js`), which a client's proposal does not need: splitting it (already on the software factory's cleanup list) would take the proposal under 350 KB.
- Proof: `node scripts/proposal-fits.js --settings <file>` renders the made-up proposal with a Solar title picture and an About picture; the fixture now has four pictures, one with no caption.

**73b as built (11 Nov 2026, LIVE 12 Nov 2026, migration 20261130100000):** points 3 to 6. Calls made in the build:
- **The tagger starts off.** Settings > Company Details > **Picture tags** has "Try it on 50 pictures (nothing is saved)", which lists what each picture would be tagged and the cost a picture, then "Switch on and tag all N pictures". Until it is switched on nothing is tagged or saved (a new AI worker starts read only). Once on, every upload path asks for the job's new pictures to be read (Site Photos, a task's photo, a marked-up copy, My Day and clock-out).
- **Tags** are lower case words on the picture: brands seen, what it shows (from a fixed list of 22 things), models read off a label, and the kind of picture. The job's Pylon equipment goes to the AI as a hint only; a brand is tagged only when it can be seen.
- **Fine for proposals** is set only for a finished install with no people and nothing identifying, and the code checks that itself.
- **Search:** the top search bar has a **Pictures** group (thumbnail, what it shows, the J or Q number); a row opens that job's Site Photos already searched. Site Photos has its own search box and the tags under each picture, with Edit to add or take one off by hand.
- **Recent installs with this gear** shows on step 4 by itself when another job has a finished install tagged with a brand on this quote (from the Pylon models and the stage lines). One tap adds a copy captioned "Similar system".
- **Model and cost:** the app's standard model (Claude Sonnet 5.5) at low effort, the picture at 1,024 px. Worked out from the price list, about half a US cent a picture, so about US$6 once for the pictures already in the app; the dry run shows the real figure before anything is saved.
- Not run here: the AI itself (no key in a cloud session). The code around it is tested with a stand-in; the eval's four picture cases run with the key (`node agents/evals/photo-tagger/run.js`).
- Its own migration (the plan had one for all of item 73): `project_photos` tags, the tags as a line of text for searching, caption, fine-for-proposals, read-at, note, tries and a claim mark, two indexes, and the on switch on `company_settings`.
- **A person has the last word on what other customers see.** Recent installs only offers pictures from jobs we won (a job number), taken after the job was approved. A tap opens the whole picture first, with "Add to this proposal" or "Not for other customers" (never offered again). The tag editor has the same tick. The code's own gate is strict: every flag has to be there, and a person, paperwork, a vehicle or a screen in shot rules a picture out.
- **A read that fails is tried again**, up to 3 times, then given up with the reason; Settings shows how many could not be read and can put them back on the list. A run where every read fails reports as failed to the ops manager (the AI is down or out of credit). Two runs can never read the same picture (a claim mark), and tags a person added before the tagger got there are kept.
- A fresh reviewer's 10 findings were fixed before the merge (the three above, a search that missed "sigenergy solar panels", brand words picked out of model names, link buttons that were white on white on the light theme, the 1,000 row limit on the Settings run, and smaller ones).
- To check before switching on: Supabase may charge for resizing each picture once (the reviewer's memory is about US$5 per 1,000 pictures above the plan's allowance; not confirmed).

Not in this plan: a different title picture per quote (one per type was the ask), AI drawing or generating pictures, tagging documents.

Done means (staging): (1) Settings > Company Details > Proposal pictures: upload a Solar title picture, open a Solar quote's link: the front page shows it; a New Build quote shows its own. (2) Upload an About picture: every proposal's About block shows it. (3) Quote > step 4 > add a picture from the phone, tap "Similar system", Save: the Your system page shows it with that caption. (4) Add a picture of a Sigenergy inverter to a job: within a minute it carries Sigenergy and inverter tags; typing Sigenergy in the search bar shows it. (5) A quote with Sigenergy equipment shows Recent installs with this gear; ticking one adds it to the proposal. (6) Step 4 > open a site picture, add an install picture, tap Cut out the equipment (or Trace around it by hand, then tidy the edge with Rub out), drag it onto the wall, Save: the new picture is on the quote captioned "Indicative picture".

Touches: `public/project.html` (step 4 pictures only, new code in its own files), `public/settings.html` (two cards, in their own file), `public/quote.html` (title, About, Your system), `public/js/supabase-client.js` (upload helper asks for tags), `public/js/global-search.js`, new `public/js/quote-pictures.js`, `public/js/photo-compose.js`, `public/js/settings-proposal-pictures.js`, new `netlify/functions/tag-photos-background.js`, `netlify/functions/_shared/photo-tagger.js`, `agents/prompts/photo-tagger.md`, `agents/evals/photo-tagger/`, one migration (`company_settings.proposal_cover_photos`, `about_photo_url`; `project_photos.tags`, `ai_caption`, `ok_for_proposals`, `tagged_at` and a tags index; a `product_pictures` table; `get_public_company_settings` returns the two new settings). Overlaps: item 72 (same `quote.html`: build 72 first, in the same chat). Kind: feature (into `staging`); migration yes; money, stock, RLS or payroll no (one new table with staff-only RLS, one public settings function redefined with the same checks). Size: large (two and a half days, in the three builds above). Test on staging: admin@staging.test for Settings, sales@staging.test for the quote; the AI key on the staging site for the tags. Cloud sessions cannot open the staging site (item 36's note), so the proof run needs `*.netlify.app` and the staging database allowed on the cloud environment, or a laptop session. Simpler: the front page shows the right picture by itself for each type; nobody names or files pictures, the AI tags them; a quote offers matching install pictures without a search; the caption says by itself when a picture is not the client's own site. Still manual: picking which pictures go on a proposal (a judgement call), placing the equipment on the wall picture, and uploading a product picture once per model where no install picture exists. Best model: Fable 5.2, effort xhigh, because it has a migration, a redefined public function and a new AI agent.

**73c as built (12 Nov 2026, LIVE the same evening, release PR 393, migration 20261201020000):** points 7 to 9, way A. Calls made in the build:
- **Where it is:** every picture in step 4's Pictures of your system has **Put equipment on this picture**. That picture is the wall. The editor (`public/js/photo-compose.js`, loaded when first pressed) opens over the page; what it saves comes back as a new picture on the quote, captioned "Indicative picture" by itself. Neither original picture is ever changed.
- **Choosing the equipment:** Product pictures first (the quote's own makes at the front), then Recent installs with this gear (the tagged pictures from 73b), then the quote's other pictures, or a picture from the phone or computer.
- **Two screens.** Place: drag the equipment with a finger or the mouse, size it with the slider or two fingers, crop the wall picture with a box. Cut out: **Trace** (draw around it and everything outside the line goes), **Box**, **Rub out** and **Bring back** (a brush, with a size slider), **Undo** (15 cuts back) and **Start again**. What has been cut away shows faintly, so Bring back has something to aim at. A product picture with a clear background skips the cut out screen.
- **Cut out automatically** is one tap on the cut out screen, shown only when the service's key is set. The service is Photoroom's Remove Background API (about 2 US cents a picture, checked 12 Nov 2026), through `netlify/functions/cut-out-picture.js` (pricing roles). Only the one picture being cut out is sent, with no quote, client or job details, and only its outline is used. In-browser tools were ruled out: 40 to 170 MB downloads, and the main one's licence (AGPL) does not suit. **Not run against the real service yet** (no account, and outside hosts are blocked in cloud sessions): the first cut with a key is the test, and any failure says so in plain words and leaves the hand tools.
- **Settings > Company Details > Product pictures** (`public/js/settings-product-pictures.js`, table `product_pictures`, Admin writes, staff read): add pictures (a PNG keeps its clear background), type the make and model under each, remove. Saved as you go. The Photoroom key goes in Settings > API Keys.
- The saved picture is up to 1,920 pixels on its long side; the equipment is worked on at up to 1,200.
- **Another job's picture is looked at first**, as on the quote's own Recent installs: the whole picture, a reminder to check nobody and nothing identifying is in it and to cut the equipment out, and "Not for other customers" to take it off the offer for good.
- A fresh reviewer's 15 findings were fixed before the merge: a second finger no longer cuts while a cut is being drawn, cropping the wall no longer moves or resizes the equipment, a crop dragged past the edge keeps only the box shown, a late automatic answer and a press during saving do nothing, two taps open one editor, the editor lets go of its pictures when it closes, a redraw of the page behind no longer loses the saved picture, a product picture's soft edges keep their strength, a gesture the phone cancels is dropped, and errors read in plain words.
- Way B (a picture-making AI) is not built.
- Touches as built: new `public/js/photo-compose.js`, new `public/js/settings-product-pictures.js`, new `netlify/functions/cut-out-picture.js`, `public/js/quote-pictures.js` (v=3, one button), `public/settings.html` (one card, one key field), `public/project.html` (`?v=` only), migration `20261201020000_product_pictures.sql`.

## 74. Client portal: builders and repeat clients see their quotes and jobs, ask for work, message us, and refer jobs for a reward (planned 11 Nov 2026, every answer in, waiting on "yes, build")

Building in: `feat/client-portal` (owner's yes 11 Nov 2026). **PR 1 built 11 Nov 2026 (PR 377 into `staging`, spec A35):** login by email and password (a temporary one emailed when a contact is ticked, changed on first login, the code as the forgot-password path; owner's change the same day), the lists, the job page, the photo and document ticks, invoices, work so far, the Clients page tick, Send login and Portal off, and the usage log (owner's add the same day: every login and open recorded, the Clients card, the job activity log and the digest). Still to build: PR 2 (requests for work with due dates and suggested dates, messages on the Feed tab, the People card, the portal link in client emails and on the proposal and invoice pages), PR 3 (the referral link and lead rewards, staff links and KPIs, the review ask on site, the Rewards settings and the two scheme documents).

Owner's ask (11 Nov 2026): a page for the builders and clients who use us often, where they log in and see their quotes and jobs and what each is up to, request quotes and book in work, see the progress of a quote or job, see when we are scheduled, see whether anyone is clocked in on one of their jobs right now, see only the photos we have ticked for them, see no quote pricing until we have sent the quote, see our Schedule of Rates and any other documents we allow, and on do and charge jobs see the hours logged so far. Owner's answers (11 Nov 2026, second round) are folded in below and marked **Owner**.

**What exists today (surveyed 11 Nov 2026):** there is no client login of any kind. Clients only ever get token links: the proposal (`quote.html?token=`, `get_quote_by_token`), a fixed variation (`quote.html?variation=`), the invoice (`invoice.html?token=`, `get_invoice_by_token_v3`, Pay online), pick a time (`pick-time.html`), the review page, the Schedule of Rates and the commercial agreement letter (`rates.html`). Every row-level security policy assumes a logged-in user is staff (`to authenticated using (true)` on `projects`, `project_photos`, `project_documents`, `schedule_assignments`, `activity_log`, `profiles` and more), and `profiles.role` allows only admin, finance, sales and staff. So a client can never be an ordinary login: the portal works the way the token pages do, through server code that returns only what that client may see. No photo or document has a client-visible flag today. Hours on a job are `time_entries` (clock in, clock out, `billable`), a do and charge job is `proposal_template = 'direct_job'`, `get-invoiceable-actuals.js` already prices a do and charge job's labour (Schedule of Rates at the client's agreement, split into bands) and materials (cost plus the stage's markup), purchase orders carry `status` ordered, received, invoiced, paid, `get_active_clock_ins()` answers who is on site now, and the job's Feed tab (`feed_posts` with `project_id`, `feed_comments`, `feed_post_photos`) is where a message can sit.

**Plan:**

1. **Who gets in.** **Owner:** we tick "Portal" on a client's contacts, and the client's main email can let others in. So: `client_contacts.portal_access` (ticked on the Clients page; the client's main email, `clients.email`, is always in); a person logged in with the main email sees a People card where they add or remove emails (each becomes a contact with the tick, shown to staff on the Clients page, removable by staff). **Owner:** a site contact on a job (`job_contacts` with an email, a builder's supervisor) can log in too and sees that one job only: progress, bookings, who is on site, shared photos and documents, messages; never invoices, hours, costs, purchase orders or the Schedule of Rates (money stays with the people on the client record). Any client, residential too (**Owner**). A staff email is refused and told to use the app. "Portal off" on the Clients page (one button, a reason) shuts a client out and ends every session of theirs at once.
2. **Login by email and password (owner, 11 Nov 2026, replacing the code-only login).** Username is the email; a temporary password is emailed by itself when a contact is ticked Portal (and from Send login on the card or Send portal login on a job contact); the first login asks for their own; the email code stays as the forgot-password path and always ends on Choose your password; a usage log on our end (logins, views, opens) on the Clients card, the job's activity log and the digest. As first planned: The person types their email, gets a 6 digit code from sales@ (`portal-login.js`, the Gmail sending every client email uses, not recorded on a job), types it, and that device stays logged in for 30 days (a code again after that, or on a new device). No password exists, so nobody can reuse a leaked one and nobody at Thomson Energy resets anything; "Log out" and "Log out everywhere" on the page. Tables `portal_logins` (email, code hash, expires, attempts, used) and `portal_sessions` (token hash, email, client ids, created, last seen, expires, device, ended). One email on two clients sees both with a switch at the top.
3. **One page, `public/portal.html` and `public/js/portal.js`**, phone first, the proposal page's look. Top: "Needs you" (a quote to approve, a variation to sign, a time to pick, an invoice overdue, a message from us), each a button to the existing token page or the job. Then **Jobs** (open first, finished behind "Show finished") and **Quotes**, each row with the number, name, site, and one plain status line from `pipeline_stage` and the bookings: "Quote being prepared, due Thu 20 Nov", "Quote sent, waiting on you", "Approved, deposit to pay", "Being booked", "Booked for Tue 18 Nov, Cooper and Guy", "On site now: Cooper since 7:42am", "Work finished, invoice to come", "Invoice sent", "Paid", "Complete". Internal stages (Awaiting Our Action, Ready to Invoice) never show as such.
4. **A job's page** (`portal.html?job=`): the status line, the site, the site manager's first name and the office number (never a tradie's mobile), the next bookings (date, start and finish, first names; only bookings marked Client informed, tentative ones stay hidden by the rule that already exists), who is on site now, the ticked photos, the shared documents, the messages (point 8), the invoices with status and Pay online. **Owner:** a quoted job shows progression only. A do and charge job (`direct_job`, and do and charge variations on a quoted job) also shows **Work so far**: labour as one line per clocked day (first name, hours, standard or after hours, $ at the Schedule of Rates with their agreement, invoiced yet or not), materials used so far at the price they would be invoiced (cost plus the stage's markup, the same maths as `get-invoiceable-actuals.js`, reused not copied), and the job's purchase orders (PO number, supplier, what is on it, total at the client's price, and "Ordered, waiting for pickup", "Picked up", "Invoiced"), with a running "About $X not yet invoiced". Our supplier cost price never appears.
5. **Quotes** link to the proposal once sent (`sent_at` set); before that the row says "Being prepared" with the due date. A draft quote's prices are never returned by the portal code, not just hidden on the page.
6. **Photos the client may see.** **Owner:** hidden until ticked. `project_photos.client_visible` (false by default), a tick per photo on the job's Photos tab and "Share ticked with client"; a shared photo shows a small eye. Proposal cover and reference photos are already the client's.
7. **Documents the client may see.** `project_documents.client_visible` (false by default), a tick per document on the Documents tab. Visible by itself, no tick needed: anything we have already sent them (the quote PDF once sent, every invoice PDF, signed variations, the commercial agreement letter), anything they uploaded themselves, and the Schedule of Rates page at their agreement (`get_schedule_of_rates`, `get_commercial_agreement`). Internal uploads (plans with notes, supplier bills, markups, bill readings) stay hidden unless ticked.
8. **Messages on the job.** **Owner:** they land on the job's Feed tab, shown apart from team chat; and we can tick "Email client" (and later "Text client") on a post so it reaches them and shows in the portal too. So: `feed_posts.client_visible` (true on a post from the client, and on ours when the tick is on), `feed_posts.from_client_email`, `feed_posts.sent_to_client_at`. The Feed tab shows client-visible posts in their own strip with a Client badge; a ticked post emails the client through the usual sending (recorded on the job) and the portal shows the thread. A client's post notifies the site manager and the office like a feed mention does. Text comes with item 7 (Twilio).
9. **Request work.** **Owner:** quotes and do and charge; a quote request becomes a draft quote at once, with a due date the client sets, and they can keep adding information and documents to it; we can extend the due date when we cannot make it. A do and charge request lets them suggest booking dates and we confirm. So one form: which site (their saved sites, or a new address, saved as a site), what they need, photos and documents, and either **Quote it** (plus "Needed by", `projects.client_due_date`) or **Book it in** (plus one to three suggested dates, `projects.client_suggested_dates`; offered only to a client with a commercial agreement or a do and charge job already). Quote it draws the next Q number through the same code `new-project.html` uses, client and site linked, their words as the pre-quote notes, their files in a "From client" folder (`project_documents`, visible to them by itself), and notifies the office; the Quotes page shows "From the portal, due 20 Nov"; while it is a draft the client can add more notes and files from the portal (never edit ours). **Extend:** on step 1 a pricing role sets a new due date with a reason; the client sees "Now due Mon 24 Nov" and gets an email. Book it in draws the next J number as a do and charge job in Awaiting Our Action with "Requested by client" and the dates; the Schedule tab shows "Client asked for Tue 18 or Wed 19" as chips, booking one of them and marking Client informed sends the confirmation exactly as today, and the portal flips to "Booked". Nothing new for staff beyond the chips and the due date.
10. **What the portal never shows:** our supplier cost prices, margins, wages, supplier bills, stock, internal notes, time entry notes, incidents, team chat, feed posts not ticked, staff surnames and mobiles, other clients' anything, tentative bookings, draft quote prices, hours on fixed price jobs, bill readings beyond what the proposal already prints, the AI's summaries.
11. **One door, server side.** **Owner:** check security, this could be a weak point. So every portal read and write goes through Netlify functions, never anon table access and no new anon-callable SQL functions: `portal-login.js` (send code, check code, log out), `portal-read.js` (overview, job, work so far), `portal-write.js` (request, message, upload, people). All three call `_shared/portal-session.js` first (one place that checks the token, the client scope, the rate limits and writes the log) and `_shared/portal-data.js` (the whitelisted queries). The staff side (ticks, extend, chips) is ordinary RLS work by pricing roles. Every login, failed code, request, upload and message is written to `activity_log` against the client; the Clients page shows "Last in the portal: 3 Nov" and the ops digest counts failed logins.

**Security check (11 Nov 2026), the weak points and what the design does about each:**

- **Guessing a code.** 6 digits, 10 minutes, one live code per email, 5 wrong tries then the code is dead and a new one is needed, at most 3 codes per email per hour and 20 per IP per hour, the code stored hashed. The reply is the same whether or not the email is known ("If that email is on a client record, a code is on its way"), so nobody can list our clients by trying emails.
- **Stealing a session.** The device holds a 32 byte random token; the table holds only its hash; 30 days then a code again; "Log out everywhere" ends every session; Portal off ends them for the client; every session row records the device and last seen. No token ever goes in a link or an email (the portal link in emails is plain `/portal.html`).
- **Seeing another client's data.** The scope is worked out on the server from the session (client ids from the contact tick, job ids from `job_contacts`), every query carries it, every field is named on a whitelist, and the smoke run proves three things on every build: a draft quote returns no prices, another client's job id returns nothing, a job contact's call returns no money fields.
- **Our prices leaking.** Materials and purchase orders show the client's price only, worked out by the same shared code the invoice uses; `unit_cost`, supplier bill lines and margins are never selected.
- **Public photo bucket.** `site-photos` is a public bucket today (anyone with a URL can open the photo, the paths are random). The portal lists ticked photos only, which is the same exposure every quote page has now. Hardening for a later item: a private bucket with short signed links.
- **Client uploads and text.** Files go only through `portal-write.js` into the private `project-documents` bucket under `portal/<client id>/`, images and PDFs only, 20 MB each, 10 per request, names escaped; every message and note goes through `escapeHtml()` wherever it is shown, 2,000 characters, 20 posts an hour. A client's text never reaches an AI action or a push to the whole team: the site manager and the office get the notice.
- **Abuse of the forms.** A request or a message is rate limited per session and per client; a shut-out client gets "Portal off" on every call; staff emails are refused.
- **Who can let people in.** Only the main email (`clients.email`) can add other emails, each addition is logged and visible to staff on the Clients page, and staff can untick anyone. Nobody can add themselves.
- **Existing staff rules untouched.** No policy changes, no new `to authenticated` rows, `enable_signup` stays as it is because the portal never creates an auth user.

**74b. Lead rewards: a client sends us a lead and gets a payment or a credit when we win it (owner's add, 11 Nov 2026).** **Owner:** some clients get an option to be paid or credited for a lead. They fill in a form in the portal (photos of the roof, switchboard or fault; the person's details; the job type, with questions we build per type: solar, battery, switchboard upgrade, fault find and so on) and send it through. We quote it; $1k to $5k they get $80, $5k to $15k a $200 credit, over $15k a $500 credit, nothing under $1k; and when we quote, the reward is hidden inside the cost.

1. **Who gets the option.** `clients.lead_reward_kind`: off (default), **pay** (bank transfer) or **credit** (taken off their next invoice), set on the Clients page. That switch is the owner's ask (some clients, not all). The tiers are data, not constants: `company_settings.lead_reward_tiers` (**Owner, 11 Nov 2026:** nothing under $1k, then `[{from: 1000, amount: 80}, {from: 5000, amount: 200}, {from: 15000, amount: 500}]`, for a full referral), `lead_reward_quick_amount` (a quick referral, $50), `staff_link_amount` ($50), `staff_site_lead_amount` ($0), `staff_under_budget_amount` ($100) and `lead_reward_min_job` ($1,000), editable under Settings > Company Details.
2. **The form is a link, no login.** **Owner (11 Nov 2026):** as easy as possible so they remember to do it. So each client with the option on gets a personal referral link, `refer.html?r=<clients.referral_token>`, that opens the form straight away: no code, no login, the referrer known from the link. They keep it on the phone's home screen, text it to their own staff, or stick the QR on the van. When the option is turned on, the client gets one email with the link, a QR card to print and a line to forward ("Know someone who needs solar, a switchboard or a fault fixed? Send it here"); the portal has the same button, and every job handover email to a rewarded client carries the link, so the reminder comes by itself. The link can only ever send a lead in that client's name (it reads nothing), 10 referrals an hour per link, and a junk lead costs nothing because the reward only ever pays on a job we win; Regenerate link on the Clients page if one gets passed around. Their referrals list (money on it) stays behind the portal login. The form: the job type (Solar, Battery, Switchboard upgrade, Fault find, Renovation, Service work, Other), then that type's questions, the person's name, phone, email and the site address, photos (roof, switchboard, the fault, through `compressImage()` into the private `lead-uploads` bucket), and a line they tick: "I have told this person Thomson Energy will contact them". The first question is **Who is this for?**: **Me** (their own job: it becomes their draft quote exactly as Quote it in point 9 of the portal plan, with a Needed by date) or **Someone else** (a referral). **Owner (11 Nov 2026): the link works for a business referral too, not always an inspection; a referral without the inspection earns a flat $50 or so.** So a referral is one of two kinds: **Quick** (who they are, their number, what they need, a line about it: 30 seconds, for a business contact or a name passed on at the pub) at a flat amount, `company_settings.lead_reward_quick_amount` ($50 to start, editable), or **Full** (the type's questions and the photos: the inspection) at the tiers. The form says the two amounts beside the choice so they know which to pick. The lead carries `leads.referral_kind` (`quick` or `full`) and the Quotes page shows it; a quick referral still earns nothing when the quote is under $1k. Three screens on a phone, under a minute. **Owner (11 Nov 2026):** once they fill in the link it shows on their portal as a quote. So both paths land in the portal's Quotes list straight away: their own job as a Q row with the usual status words; a referral as a "Referred: J. Smith, switchboard" row that gets its Q number once the office creates the quote, then "Being quoted", "Quoted", "Won: $200 credit once the deposit is paid", "Credit due", "Credited on INV-1234" or "Not won". A referral row never links to the proposal and never shows the price or the person's address beyond the suburb (it is someone else's quote). The questions are the plain-English subset of the quote's own type questions in `public/js/template-questions.js` (each question gains a `lead: true` mark, so a type's questions live in one place); Switchboard upgrade and Fault find are two new short sets on the Service work type. It lands as a lead (`leads.referrer_client_id`, `source = 'portal_referral'`, answers in `details`, photos in `details.attachments` as the website's do) through `portal-write.js` (the token checked there, never direct table access), the office is notified as for any lead, and Create quote from it carries the answers onto `projects.template_answers`, so nobody retypes them. The Quotes page's Pending leads shows "Referred by ABC Homes".
3. **The reward on the quote.** `projects.referrer_client_id` is set at Create quote from the lead. The first stage gets one line "Lead reward (ABC Homes)" with `cost_centre_line_items.internal = true`: **Owner: at cost exactly, hidden.** An internal line is added to the stage price after the markup, dollar for dollar (the markup never touches it), and is never printed on the proposal, the PDF, the breakdown, the invoice or the job's cost view for staff who are not pricing roles (`get_quote_by_token`, the quote PDF, the breakdown and `create-job-from-quote.js` carry it as internal; the stage maths in `public/js/supabase-client.js` and `_shared/invoice-items.js` add internal lines after markup). Its amount follows the tier of the quote's total before the line (**Owner: the total out of pocket to the client**: inc GST, after the STC and BSTC credits, the figure the proposal shows as payable), worked out again by the database whenever the lines change (`lead_reward_sync` trigger on `cost_centre_line_items`), so the Summary chip "Referred by ABC Homes: $200 credit if won" is always right and nobody adjusts it by hand. A tier is decided on the quote at approval and never moves after (variations do not change it).
4. **When it is owed.** `lead_rewards` (referrer_client_id, lead_id, project_id, amount, kind, status, earned_at, due_at, settled_at, invoice_id or paid_reference, created_at). **Owner (11 Nov 2026, final): owed once the deposit is paid, not at quote approval.** The row is made **earned** when the referred quote is approved (`accept-quote.js`, `manually-approve-quote.js`), the amount fixed then, and **due** when the referred job's deposit invoice is paid (or its first invoice, when the quote has no deposit), caught where payments already land (`airwallex-webhook.js`, `xero-webhook.js`, a paid date set by hand on the Invoices page). A referred quote that is declined or expires makes no row; a job that falls over before the deposit pays nothing, and the owner can cancel a row with a reason on the Lead rewards list. **Credit:** the next invoice raised to the referrer takes it by itself as a minus line "Lead credit, J7020: -$200" after any Less line (the same place `create-invoice.js` puts the commercial agreement line), marks the row settled with that invoice, and goes to Xero inside that invoice. **Pay:** a "Lead rewards" list on the Clients page (due first) with the referrer's bank details from their record, "Paid" with a reference and date, and a Xero spend money line to Advertising and Marketing (account code to confirm with the owner, it is not in `docs/BUSINESS_FACTS.md`).
5. **In the portal:** referrals sit in the Quotes list as point 2 says (no separate page to find), each with its status word and the amount, and a Rewards line at the top of the portal when anything is due or settled ("$200 credit due, taken off your next invoice").
6. **Rules that need no switch:** a referral whose phone or email matches the referrer's own record, or a client who already has an open quote or job with us at that address, earns nothing (the lead still comes through, marked "already a client"); one reward per lead; a reward is never earned twice when a quote is duplicated. The first email or call to the referred person says who passed on their details (the Privacy Act asks us to say where personal information came from).

7. **The same link for every staff member (owner's add, 11 Nov 2026).** Each person gets `profiles.referral_token` and the same form reached two ways. **In the app:** a "New lead" button on My Day and Home (the same `refer.js` form, the person already filled in; "Same customer as the job I'm on" copies the name, phone and site from the job they are clocked in to, for the customer who asks about solar while we are fixing their switchboard; photos from the phone there and then). **To send anyone:** their personal link `refer.html?s=<token>` (a friend or a neighbour fills it in, sees a thank-you, and nothing else). Both land as that person's lead (`leads.referrer_profile_id`): the Leads page shows "From Cooper", Create quote carries the answers and sets `projects.referrer_profile_id`. **KPIs:** the staff profile's KPIs card gets a Leads block worked out by the database for the period (sent, became quotes, won, won value, `staff_lead_stats(profile_id, from, to)`), and a KPI whose measure is "Leads sent" or "Leads won" shows that live figure as its result instead of a typed one, so nobody counts by hand. **Owner (11 Nov 2026, final): employees get $50, owed once the deposit is paid, for anyone they give the link to; a lead they fill in on site is KPIs only (they are paid to fill the form in); if they quoted the job too and it finishes under budget they get something as well; every amount editable in Settings.** So a staff lead from their link makes a `lead_rewards` row (kind `staff_link`, `company_settings.staff_link_amount`, $50) earned at approval and due when the deposit is paid, with the hidden line at cost on the quote like a client's; a lead entered in the app (`leads.entered_by` = the referrer) makes no money row (`staff_site_lead_amount`, $0, there if the owner ever wants it) and counts on the KPIs. **Under budget:** `projects.quoted_by` (defaults to the quote's `created_by`, changeable on step 1, carried to the job at approval). When a job moves to Complete, `job_budget_check(project_id)` compares what it cost us (labour at cost from `compute-labour-cost.js`, every material and PO line at cost) with what the quote allowed (every stage's `estimated_labour_cost` plus `estimated_material_cost`, approved variations included); at or under makes a row (kind `staff_under_budget`) for `quoted_by`. **Owner (11 Nov 2026): a percent of the saving, up to $500, and the client has to be happy, a 5 star review.** So the amount is `staff_under_budget_percent` (**2%**, owner 11 Nov 2026) of the saving (allowance less cost), capped at `staff_under_budget_cap` ($500), and the row is **earned** only when three things are true: the job is Complete and under budget, no defect or warranty job has been raised against it in its first 30 days, and the client's review for that job is at least `staff_under_budget_min_stars` (5 to start; the owner can set 4, or 0 for no review needed). **Asking for the review is one tap on site (owner, 11 Nov 2026: make it easy for the boys on site to ask):** when someone answers Complete at clock-out, the next screen is "Ask the customer for a review" with the job's review link as a big QR code to hold up (the customer scans it and taps the stars there and then, `review.html` already works on a phone) and a Send button that emails it from sales@ in that person's name (text when Twilio comes, item 7); **no clock-out needed (owner, 11 Nov 2026: they ask while packing up and clock out back at the shed):** while a person is clocked in, My Day's current job card has a Review button (**owner: My Day, not Home**), opening the same QR screen for the job they are on, any time; the clock-out screen only offers it again if nobody on the job has asked yet. **Whoever gets the review gets the credit (owner, 11 Nov 2026):** the QR code and the Send button make that person's own `review_requests` row for the job (`created_by` is already on the table; one row per person per job, the same job can have one for each person on it), so the review the customer submits is theirs. The staff profile's block becomes **Leads and reviews**: leads sent, became quotes, won and their value, then reviews asked, reviews received, 5 star reviews, average stars and how many went on to Google (`google_clicked_at`), for the period; a KPI measured as "Reviews received" or "5 star reviews" shows the live figure as its result. The job's Feed shows "Cooper got a 5 star review from Mrs Smith" and the team sees it on Home, because a review earned on site is worth a mention. The automatic request at Complete is credited to the site manager. **$5 per review (owner, 11 Nov 2026):** a review the customer submits on a person's own link makes a `lead_rewards` row (kind `staff_review`, `company_settings.staff_review_amount`, $5) earned at once, one per person per job, paid on the next pay run with the other bonuses; it is paid for any rating, not only 5 stars (paying for stars would push people to lean on customers, and the ACCC's guidance on reviews is against anything that buys or filters ratings), and never for the automatic request at Complete. Whatever they do, the link goes out by itself if nobody has sent it by the time the job moves to Complete (`sweep-job-complete-review.js`, same link as the composer makes), with one reminder after 7 days; today the link is only ever sent by hand from the email composer. The row sits as "Under budget $46 waiting on the review" on the staff profile until the review lands, and lapses after 60 days with no review (the owner can mark it earned by hand with a reason). Due when earned (no deposit step here), paid on the next pay run. The staff profile's Leads block shows each row as "Link bonus $50 due" or "Under budget $100 due" and when it was paid. **Paying it:** the fortnightly timesheet push (`push-timesheets-to-xero.js`) adds one earnings line "Lead bonus" per person for the rows due in that fortnight (a Xero pay item the owner creates once: Payroll settings > Pay items > Earnings > Add > Allowance, name "Lead bonus", type Other, taxed, super as the accountant says) and marks them settled with the pay run; until the pay item exists the push leaves the rows due and says so. **Every amount in one place:** Settings > Company Details > Rewards card (`public/js/settings-rewards.js`): the client tiers, the quick amount, the staff link amount, the on-site lead amount, the under budget amount, and the minimum job size ($1,000), all `company_settings` columns. **Shape as a setting (owner's question 11 Nov 2026, flat or a percent):** each staff bonus has a type (flat, percent of the job's total, or percent of the saving for under budget), a rate, a cap and a minimum (`company_settings.staff_bonus_rules` jsonb), so flat today can become a percent without a build. Owner's pick (11 Nov 2026): link bonus flat; under budget a percent of the saving capped at $500, with a 5 star review from the client, because the person who sets the estimate is the person paid for beating it and the review stops corner cutting. **Legal points (not advice, the adviser confirms):** a staff bonus is wages (super on it, PAYG, STP as bonuses and commissions, WorkCover wages, payroll tax wages if ever over the threshold, always on top of the award); the staff document says the scheme is discretionary, not a contract term, and can be changed or withdrawn without notice for anything not yet won (**owner, 11 Nov 2026**), while a bonus already earned is still paid because earned wages cannot be clawed back; the client document says the same for referrals; a "Scheme off" switch on the Rewards card stops new rewards the same minute and leaves earned ones to be paid; a builder's employee (a job contact) is never paid personally, their referral credits the client's account, because a personal payment for passing on their employer's work can be a secret commission under NSW and Queensland crimes law; a credit to a business is a discount on our invoice so GST adjusts itself, a cash payment to a GST-registered business needs their invoice, so businesses get credits and individuals may be paid. **Xero (owner's ask: a sales expense on the P&L):** one Expense account "Referral fees and sales bonuses" (the owner makes it: Accounting > Chart of accounts > Add account, type Expense); the Lead bonus pay item posts to it (Payroll settings > Pay items > Earnings > Lead bonus > Expense account); the client credit line on an invoice maps to it through the existing account mapping (`xero_account_mappings`, a new key `referral_fees`) so it is an expense, not less sales; client payments are Spend money to it. **Two documents (owner's ask):** drafted 11 Nov 2026 in `policies and Contracts/` as "Thomson Energy Referral Rewards (Clients).txt" and "Thomson Energy Lead and Bonus Scheme (Staff).txt" for the owner's read; the build turns them into live documents that print the amounts from Settings on the day: the client one as `generate-client-document-pdf` type `referral_rewards`, sent with the link email and shown in the portal's documents; the staff one as an onboarding document every employee signs (`onboarding_documents`, the usual sign-off), re-issued when an amount changes. A staff member's link stops working the day they are made inactive.

Owner's answers on 74b (11 Nov 2026): (1) Owed once the deposit is paid (changed from quote approval the same day). (2) The tier reads the total out of pocket to the client (inc GST, after the credits). (3) At cost exactly, hidden. (4) KPIs only for now (they are paid to fill the form in), payroll bonus later: built so both work. **Answered by the owner 11 Nov 2026, folded in above and in point 7.**

Not in this plan: a client password, clients editing our quotes or jobs, clients approving do and charge variations, SMS codes and text messages (Twilio, item 7), a native app for clients, signed photo links, paying lead rewards through Airwallex payouts (item 22, needs the payout key).

Done means (staging): (1) `portal.html`, type a seed client's ticked email, get the code, log in: the client's jobs and quotes with plain statuses; another client's job id in the address bar shows nothing. (2) On a draft quote the portal shows no prices; send it from step 5: the row now links to the proposal. (3) Tick two photos on a job and Share: the portal shows those two only. (4) Book a job and mark Client informed: the portal shows the date and first names; clock in as staff@staging.test: "On site now". (5) On a direct job with a clocked shift and a PO, Work so far shows the day's hours at the Schedule of Rates, the PO as "Ordered, waiting for pickup" at the client's price, and "not yet invoiced". (6) Quote it from the portal with a due date and a PDF: the Quotes page shows the new Q "From the portal, due ...", the PDF is on its Documents tab; extend the date from step 1: the portal shows the new date. (7) Post a message in the portal: it is on the Feed tab with a Client badge; reply with Email client ticked: the portal shows the reply and the email goes. (8) Set a seed client to Credit, open their referral link in a private window (no login) and refer a job with photos, Create quote from the lead: the answers are on step 1, the first stage carries the internal line, the Summary chip says the tier; price it over $5k: the chip says $200; approve it: the reward is earned; pay its deposit invoice on staging: due, and the referrer's next invoice shows the minus line; send a quick referral from the same link: its row says $50; the portal's Quotes list shows the referral row at every step. (9) Log in as staff@staging.test, My Day > New lead with Same customer ticked: the lead is on the Leads page as From Staff with the job's details; open the staff member's own link in a private window and send one: a second lead; their staff profile's KPIs card shows Leads sent 2; approve a quote from the staff member's link lead: the profile shows "Link bonus $50 due" and the quote carries the internal line; mark a job they quoted Complete with its costs under the estimate: the review email goes to the client and the profile says "Under budget $46 waiting on the review"; at clock-out answer Complete: the QR screen shows; scan it and give 5 stars: "Under budget $46 due", and that person's profile shows Reviews received 1, 5 star 1, "Review $5 due"; Settings > Rewards: every amount is a box.
