## 75. Site sun data for the savings estimate (built 11 Nov 2026, LIVE 12 Nov 2026, migration 20261201010000)

**As built (11 Nov 2026, owner's "Yes and use pylon api"):**
- **Nobody does anything.** When a draft Solar or Battery quote's bill card opens (and again when its address is saved), `public/js/site-sun.js` asks `netlify/functions/site-sun.js` (pricing roles) for the site's sun figures. A quote that already has figures for its address asks nothing. **A quote already sent is left alone**: its figures do not move because someone opened it or saved something else on it, only when its address changes. New quotes and copies start as drafts, so they get the records. An approved quote never asks (its step page is not shown), and the job made from it carries the quote's figures (`site_sun`, `lat`, `lng` copied in `_shared/create-job-from-quote.js`), so the job's quote PDF keeps what the client accepted.
- **Where the site is, in this order:** the Pylon site's own location (`_shared/pylon-location.js`: the quote's Pylon id is tried as a Pylon project, then as a design that names its project; `site_location`; ten seconds in all; a Pylon site in another postcode than the quote's address is not used, so a copied quote for another property is not given the first site's sun), else the address looked up once with the Places library already on the quote page (only an address that ends on a postcode, and only a match in that postcode, so a short address never lands in another town). Saved on `projects.lat` and `projects.lng` (item 4, weather, reuses them); cleared when the address changes and the new one cannot be found.
- **The records:** ten years of daily sun from NASA POWER (free, no key, no account), one fetch per one degree cell (about 100 km, the grid the records are on), kept in `sun_climate` for 400 days and kept longer if a refetch fails (`_shared/sun-climate.js`). The figures are for the area, not the roof: an area has fewer very dull days than one roof, so the clear day count can read a little high. An answer missing more than a tenth of its days, or figures the estimate would not take, is refused and tried again later, never kept.
- **Two assumed values become measured ones** (three were built, see the bug fix below) on `projects.site_sun`: kWh a day from each kW of panels, the four season factors, and (worked out, no longer used) the clear and dull days (a day under 60% of its month's average is dull; the share of those days and what they make). Sydney and Brisbane still come out at the Clean Energy Council's 3.9 and 4.2: a site with Sydney's sun or less uses Sydney's losses, with Brisbane's or more uses Brisbane's, and one in between slides between the two. The normal season spread is taken as right for Sydney and shifted by how the site's seasons differ from Sydney's in the records.
- **Used as the normal values** on that quote, on the staff page, the size suggestion and the client's proposal alike (`get_quote_by_token` passes `site_sun`; no lat or lng goes to the client's page). The proposal's Sun line reads "from the sun records for your area (2016 to 2025)" and the clear days line gives the dull day figure. Savings assumptions shows them as the normal values, says where they came from, and a typed value still wins. Figures are only used for the address they were worked out for and only when every one is believable (`roiSiteSun` in `solar-roi.js`); anything else, or any failure, keeps the area figure from item 72c and the panel says there are no sun records for the site yet.
- **First live run, 12 Nov 2026 (Q1020, the Northern Rivers cell, the address looked up):** the records service answered and the figures were kept: 4.95 kWh of sun a day for the area against Sydney's 4.67 and Brisbane's 5.15, so **4.08 kWh a day from each kW** (the area figure was 4.2), with a milder winter than Sydney's. Both read right. Pylon's site location has not had a live run yet (that quote has no Pylon id).
- **The clear and dull days are NOT taken from the records (bug fix, LIVE 12 Nov 2026, release PR 393).** The same run counted 1 dull day in 10 (a dull day making 45%) where the estimate assumes 3 in 10 at 35%. The records average an area about 100 km across, which hides a single roof's dull days, and using them would have lifted every battery quote's saving. No client saw a figure on them: the one quote that picked them up has no bill, so no savings figures. The estimate keeps its own clear and dull days (a typed value still wins); the two figures are still worked out and kept on `site_sun`, unused. So **the 72c question (7 or 8 clear days in 10) is still the owner's call**; a roof's own daily records (the Bureau's station data) could answer it, an area average cannot.
- **Pylon's own production figure: not possible through the API.** Pylon's API pages were read (11 Nov 2026): a solar design has its size, equipment, pricing and links, and no production, tilt or direction. So Pylon is used for where the site is, nothing more. Reading the production figure off Pylon's proposal PDF (the link is saved) is still open as a later step, not built.
- **Not proven live yet:** this cloud session cannot reach `power.larc.nasa.gov` or `api.getpylon.com`, so the live records answer and Pylon's location call were tested with stand-ins only (`node scripts/site-sun-check.js` for the maths; `--live` prints the real figures for Sydney, Brisbane and any `lat,lng` once the host is allowed). First check, now on the live site (released 12 Nov 2026 before a staging run, the owner's call): open a draft Solar quote with an address and a bill, wait a few seconds, and What we assumed should name the sun records. If Pylon's answer is shaped differently the function log says what came back and the address lookup is used. On staging the Google Maps key has to allow the staging site's address for the lookup to work. Look at the clear days in 10 and the dull day figure for the home area on that first run: they move a battery quote's saving the most.
- Touches as built: `public/project.html` (two small hooks, `?v=` bumps), `public/quote.html` (passes `site_sun`, `?v=`), `public/js/solar-roi.js` (v=19), `public/js/solar-sizing.js` (v=3), `public/js/quote-savings-assumptions.js` (v=3), new `public/js/site-sun.js`, new `netlify/functions/site-sun.js`, `_shared/sun-climate.js`, `_shared/pylon-location.js`, `netlify/functions/read-electricity-bill.js`, `_shared/create-job-from-quote.js`, new `scripts/site-sun-check.js`, migration `20261201010000_site_sun.sql`.

The plan as written before the build:

Owner's ask (11 Nov 2026): "Can the PSH be calculated from the postcode or pulled from Pylon? Same goes for cloudy days: pull it from Pylon, postcode or BOM. I wanted to have weather data for jobs so could use it for quotes."

What is there today:
- The sun figure is one number per STC zone, with 4.2 for Queensland and Northern Rivers postcodes (item 72c). The four season factors and the "7 clear days in 10" are the same everywhere.
- **Pylon:** the sync (`pylon-sync.js`) asks Pylon for equipment and links only, and what it stores has no production figure (`pylon_data.summary`: description, storage_kwh, dc_output_kw and five links, among them the proposal PDF). The code's own note says Pylon's API shows no production figures, never checked against a live answer. Pylon's API pages cannot be read from a cloud session (`app.getpylon.com` is blocked there).
- **Weather (item 4):** designed 28 Oct 2026, not built. Its source, Willy Weather, gives forecasts and warnings for scheduling, not long-run sun records, so it would not feed a quote. It does plan `projects.lat` and `projects.lng` from the address picker, which this needs too.
- **The Bureau:** its daily sun grids (5 km, 1990 on) are files to download, with no simple free API found.

Plan:
1. **The job's location:** save lat and long when the address is picked (the Places picker is already on the page), and work it out once from the address for quotes that have none. Item 4 reuses the same two columns.
2. **Ten years of daily sun for that spot** from NASA's POWER records (free, no key, worldwide, 1981 on, a grid of about 55 km), fetched by the server once per area and kept in a small table (`sun_climate`), so a suburb is only ever fetched once.
3. **Three assumed values become measured ones:** the sun figure (the year's average), the four season factors (the site's own summer and winter), and the clear and dull days (how many days fall well under the month's average, and how much they make). The losses factor is set so Sydney and Brisbane land on the Clean Energy Council's 3.9 and 4.2, then each site moves with its own sun.
4. **Used as the normal values** on that quote (staff page and the client's proposal alike, so `get_quote_by_token` passes them). The proposal says "from 10 years of sun records for your area". The Savings assumptions page still lets a person change any of them for one quote.
5. **Pylon's own figure, when it can be read** (phase 2): Pylon's design knows the tilt, the direction and the shading, so its yearly kWh beats any area average. Two ways to get it, to be settled by trying: Pylon's API (needs its docs), or reading Pylon's proposal PDF, whose link is already saved, the way the bill reader reads a bill. When a quote has Pylon's figure the estimate uses it and says so.

Done means (staging): (1) a Solar quote at a Queensland address: What we assumed reads "Sun: X kWh a day from each kW of panels, from 10 years of sun records for your area". (2) The same quote moved to a Melbourne address shows a lower figure and a deeper winter. (3) Savings assumptions shows those as the normal values and still takes a typed one. (4) A quote with no address keeps the zone figure and says so. (5) The three live quotes' before and after are in the PR.

Touches: `public/project.html` (the address picker saves lat and long: a small hook), `public/js/solar-roi.js`, `public/js/solar-roi-staff.js`, `public/js/quote-savings-assumptions.js`, `public/quote.html`, new `netlify/functions/site-sun.js` and `netlify/functions/_shared/sun-climate.js`, one migration (`projects.lat`, `lng`, `site_sun`; a `sun_climate` table with RLS; `get_quote_by_token` passes `site_sun`). Overlaps: item 4 (the same lat and long columns: built here, reused there), PR 376 (item 73b, other files), item 73c (next in this chat). Kind: feature (into `staging`); migration yes; money, stock or payroll no, RLS yes (one new table). Size: medium (a day). Test on staging: sales@staging.test, a Solar quote with a bill read and an address. To test from a cloud session `power.larc.nasa.gov` has to be allowed in the environment's network settings. Simpler: nobody picks or checks a sun figure, a season or cloudy days: the job's own location sets them. No new switch or step. Best model: Fable, effort xhigh, because it is a migration, a new table with RLS and the savings maths in one go.

## 76. Inbox on the whole screen: Gmail's layout, Outlook's reading pane (built and LIVE 12 Nov 2026, release PR 401, spec A18)

**Owner, 12 Nov 2026:** make the inbox more user friendly, copy Gmail's layout and use the whole screen. A long chain means scrolling a long way to reply: show the newest email first and the reply at the top, like Outlook (the new Outlook's reading pane is newest on top by default, older messages folded to one line).

**What is wrong today:** the inbox sits in a card on a page with a title, subtitle and side margins (a third of a laptop screen is frame); rows are 4 to 6 lines tall (about 8 on screen); the reading pane scrolls with the page so the reply box is far down; Drafts is a button and a slide-over panel.

**Plan (no database change, every feature kept):**
1. Whole screen: no title, subtitle, card or margins; the inbox fills the window below the top bar like Team chat, each column scrolling on its own.
2. Left column (Gmail): big Compose button, the Sales inbox / My inbox switch, then Priority, Other, Subscriptions and updates, Drafts, Sent, Done, All with counts, then Labels, Tagged to jobs, Clean up, Sync now as a small link. Drafts becomes a folder in the list; the Drafts button and panel go.
3. Middle column (the list): a search box (subject, sender, client name, words in the email, the whole inbox), Unread only and Mine as chips, two short lines per row (bold when unread: name and time, then subject, label chips and the status in colour; 10:42 am today, else 11 Nov; built as one line first, but beside a reading pane the subject and status fought for space), hover shows Done and Read or Unread. About twice as many rows on screen.
4. Right column (Outlook): subject and the action bar (Reply, I've got this, Assign, Move to, Done, Open in Gmail) stuck at the top; the reply box under it, one line until clicked, an AI draft sitting there ready; the newest email open in full under that with From, To and date; older emails below, newest to oldest, folded to one line each, click to open in place. Threading is untouched: the server answers the newest message by date whatever the screen order (`send-gmail.js`).
5. Phone: list then conversation with the back swipe as today, edge to edge, two-line rows.

**Done means:** (1) Inbox on a laptop fills the window edge to edge, three columns, no card. (2) Priority shows about 18 two-line rows without scrolling. (3) Typing a client's name in search narrows the list. (4) A long conversation shows the reply box and the newest email without scrolling, older emails folded below, a click opens one in place (drawn then; the thread's emails load in one query). (5) Drafts in the left column shows the drafts in the list and a draft opens its conversation. (6) Phone: full-width two-line rows, a conversation on its own screen, back swipe returns.

Touches (as built): `public/inbox.html`, `public/js/inbox-cleanup.js` (new, Clean up loaded on demand), `netlify/functions/email-actions.js` and `_shared/mailboxes.js` (`mark_unread`, Gmail mirrored), `docs/PROJECT_SPEC.md` A18, `docs/IDEAS_BACKLOG.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`; `style.css` untouched. Overlaps: none (PR 377 touches `list-staff-emails.js` only). Built in `feat/inbox-whole-screen`, PR 399, 12 Nov 2026. Kind: feature into `staging`, no migration, no money, stock, RLS or payroll. Size: medium. Test on staging: sales@staging.test, Inbox, the six Done means on a laptop and once on a phone, no key needed. Simpler: nobody scrolls the page to reach the reply box, opens a panel to find drafts, or scrolls tall rows to find a conversation. Best model: Opus 5.5, effort high.

## 77. Whole screen on every page (owner's rule 12 Nov 2026; whole-site plan 12 Nov 2026, waiting on yes)

**Owner, 12 Nov 2026:** make it a rule that we always use the whole screen, so there is no wasted page. Rather than scrolling through so much, put things next to each other: the job Summary tab could have the tasks far left and the summary in the middle. **Later the same day:** fix all the pages at once: fit the whole screen, minimise scrolling, size everything only as big as it needs to be, and columns left to right rather than one thing stretched across the whole screen.

The rule is in `CLAUDE.md` (How the owner works, "Use the whole screen") and the `/code-structure` skill. Inbox (item 76) was the first page, live 12 Nov 2026.

**Found (survey of all 60 pages, 12 Nov 2026):** 55 pages sit in `.wrap` (1100px wide, or 1400px with `wide`) centred with 32px padding, so a laptop shows a strip down the middle with empty sides. No page except Chat and Inbox splits into columns that scroll on their own: every page stacks full-width cards and the whole window scrolls. Worst for scrolling: Timesheets (nine cards stacked), Overheads, STCs, Leads, Stock, Bugs, Ops, Tasks, Knowledge, Logs, My Day, Prebuilds, the job page. Sizes are oversized for a laptop: topbar 76px, card padding 22px with 20px between cards, buttons 10px by 18px, table cells 10px tall padding, stat tiles 24px numbers.

**Plan: four PRs in one chat, in order, each into `staging`, no migration.**

**PR 1, the frame and the sizes (every page at once, `style.css` only plus the `?v=` bump).**
- `.wrap` and `.wrap.wide` lose their width cap and centring: 16px by 20px padding, edge to edge. `.wrap.narrow` (720px) for the pages that are reading or signing: Onboarding, Policy update, Site inspection. Pages with their own inline width keep it (login 420, portal 720, pick-time 640, rates and the client documents 860).
- Practical sizes on a laptop: topbar 60px (logo 40px), card padding 14px and 12px between cards, h1 20px and h2 15px, subtitle gap 12px, tables 13px with 6px cell padding, stat tiles 10px padding with 20px numbers, inputs 7px by 10px, buttons 7px by 14px, labels 10px above, grid gap 12px. Phones keep what they have: the touch rules (44px buttons, 16px inputs) stay as they are.
- One shared column frame, used by every page from PR 2 on: `.page-cols` is a grid under the topbar as tall as the window (`100dvh` minus the topbar, like `.inbox-app`), each `.page-col` scrolls on its own, a `.page-head` strip above it holds the title and the buttons on one line. On a phone the frame stacks to one column and the page scrolls as now. Column widths are set per page with a class (`cols-3-1`, `cols-1-1`, `cols-side` for a 300px rail and so on), nothing new per page in CSS.
- Done means: (1) Jobs on a laptop runs edge to edge with no empty sides. (2) Every page's topbar is shorter and the first row of a table is on screen without scrolling. (3) On a phone, buttons are still finger sized and nothing has moved. (4) Login, the portal, a proposal link and an invoice link look exactly as before.

**PR 2, the job page (`project.html`, class changes only, no new inline code; its budget goes down).**
- The job header (name, client, status) and the tab bar stay put at the top; the search box moves onto the header line.
- Summary tab: Tasks far left (300px), the summary cards in the middle in two columns, the Activity log on the right (320px); each column scrolls on its own.
- Every other tab: the tab's content in the middle, the Activity log on the right, so the log is always in reach.
- Quote tab: the steps bar becomes a left rail (the five steps with their ticks), the step's cards in the middle, and the Quote and client card plus Send and the ready-to-send check on the right; the tall text boxes (scope, terms) sit side by side in step 4; the STC and BSTC credits and the payment schedule sit beside the stages in step 3.
- Done means: (1) J7002 Summary: Tasks, summary and Activity log side by side, the Tasks list scrolls without the header moving. (2) Switch tab: the Activity log stays on the right. (3) A quote: the five steps on the left, click 3 Pricing and the stages are there with the credits beside them. (4) On a phone: tabs and cards stacked as now, Tasks first.

**PR 3, the pages people sit on longest, each on the frame.**
- Tasks: three columns, Assigned to you, Your role, Up for grabs.
- Leads: Call requests, Enquiries, Site interest (folded by default); the lead opens in the third column instead of a popup.
- Timesheets: My week, Team week (with lunch breaks under it), then Leave and approvals and Push to Xero in the third.
- Stock: Materials (the big table) on the left, Stock checks and Stock purchase orders on the right.
- My Day: Clock and Your schedule on the left, Your scheduled tasks in the middle, Free tasks on the right.
- Home: the shortcuts at the practical size on one line, My schedule and Needs attention on the left, the Company feed on the right scrolling on its own.
- Clients: the list on the left, the client's details and sites on the right, no tab switch.
- Settings: the tab rail stays on the left, the panel scrolls on its own, and the long panels (My Profile, Company Details, API Keys, Xero Mapping, Users and Roles) lay their cards two across.
- Schedule: the calendar fills the window height (month cells grow to fit six weeks), the day board's job search stays on the right.
- Pipeline: the board runs edge to edge so more stages fit before sideways scrolling, columns as tall as the window.
- Done means: (1) Tasks: three lists side by side, no page scroll. (2) Leads: click an enquiry and it opens on the right. (3) Timesheets: this week's hours, the team's week and the leave approvals all on screen at once. (4) Settings > My Profile: cards two across, the rail stays while the panel scrolls. (5) Schedule month view: six weeks fit without scrolling the page.

**PR 4, the rest, each on the frame.**
- Two columns: Logs (logins, activity), STCs (jobs, Formbay payments, the stats on one line above), Prebuilds (library, editor), Knowledge (library, add and edit), Documents (library, add), Vehicle stock (stock, purchase orders), Team (people, insurance and licences), Letter (the letter, the sign card), Purchase order (head and lines, then notes, bills and log), Supplier detail (bills, then purchase orders and activity), Meetings (list, the open meeting), Supplier bills and Price lists (the list, the open item), New job (the three cards across).
- Three columns: Bugs (new report and Open, In progress, Resolved), Ops (ask and Needs me, digest, workers and what they did).
- List pages with one table (Jobs, Quotes, Suppliers, Purchase orders, Invoices, Assets, Tools, Fleet, Incidents, DNSP, Reviews, Bill checks, Support): the stats, search and filters on one line in the head strip, the table in one column that scrolls on its own, so the filters never scroll away. Staff and Incident keep their tabs, cards two across.
- Overheads: edge to edge (the people table no longer needs its sideways scroll on most laptops), the sections stay in order because they are a form filled top to bottom.
- Done means: (1) Bugs: three lists side by side. (2) Jobs: scroll the list and the search box stays. (3) Purchase order PO2000: lines on the left, log on the right. (4) Letter: the letter and the sign box side by side.

**Left alone (clients, the public, print):** login, index, portal, pick-time, share-licences, review, rates, quote (the proposal), invoice, po (the supplier's copy), and Chat and Inbox (already on the whole screen).

**Lesson from item 76 kept:** nothing merges until someone has loaded the page with real data on the preview or staging (the owner, or a laptop chat); the cloud session cannot reach staging, so each PR's Done means is the owner's clicks on the preview link.

Touches: `public/css/style.css` (the frame, the sizes, `?v=25` on all 59 pages), `public/project.html` (classes only), then the pages named above in PRs 3 and 4 (markup and their page-top CSS, no logic), `scripts/page-budgets.json` only downwards, `docs/PROJECT_SPEC.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`. Overlaps: item 62 (pipeline, waiting on yes) and item 47 touch `project.html` and `dashboard.html` logic, not layout; item 74 PR 2 (client portal, to come) adds to the job Feed tab and People card; no unmerged branch or open PR touches `style.css` (checked 12 Nov 2026, only PR 34 open). Kind: feature into `staging`, four PRs; no migration; no money, stock, RLS or payroll. Size: large (two to three days; PR 1 a morning, PR 2 a day, PR 3 a day, PR 4 a morning). Test on staging: admin@staging.test, the Done means above on a laptop and once on a phone, no key needed. Simpler: nobody scrolls past tasks to read a summary, past a form to reach a list, or past a call request to reach an enquiry; what matters most on every page is on screen when it opens; no toggle or manual step added. ## 78. AI draft replies sign off twice (bug, found 12 Nov 2026, built the same day, spec A18)

**Owner, 12 Nov 2026:** the email draft still signs off with Kind regards, which the signature already says; the Writing style setting was changed to drop it and the drafts still do it.

**Cause:** the draft rule in `netlify/functions/_shared/email-agent.js` says to end every draft with "Kind regards,", and the signature added on sending (`_shared/build-email-signature.js`) starts with "Kind regards," too, so a sent AI draft reads it twice. The Writing style guide is appended after that rule and told it only wins "on wording", so the hard rule stands.

**Fix:** (1) the draft rule becomes: stop after the last answer line, no sign-off, the sender's "Kind regards," name and signature are added on sending (as `_shared/schedule-emails.js` already says). (2) On sending (`_shared/send-recorded-email.js`), a trailing sign-off line ("Kind regards,", "Regards,", "Cheers,", "Thanks,"), on its own or followed by the sender's name, is dropped before the signature goes on: covers drafts already saved, replies typed with a sign-off out of habit, and the Draft reply button. Nothing changes in Settings.

**Done means:** (1) Draft reply on a Priority email ends on the last answer line. (2) Sent, the email in Sent shows one "Kind regards," then the signature. (3) A reply typed by hand ending "Kind regards, Jasper" sends with one "Kind regards".

Touches: `netlify/functions/_shared/email-agent.js`, `netlify/functions/_shared/send-recorded-email.js`, `docs/PROJECT_SPEC.md` A18, `docs/DECISIONS.md`. Overlaps: none. Kind: bug fix into `main`, no migration, no money. Size: small. Test on staging: sales@staging.test, Inbox, Draft reply, Send reply, Sent (the draft step needs the AI key on staging). Simpler: nobody deletes a doubled sign-off before sending. Best model: Sonnet 5.5, effort medium.

## 79. AI one-shot answers cut off or empty (bug, found and fixed 12 Nov 2026, LIVE straight to `main`)

The owner saw Generate scope of works stop mid-sentence ("We supply and install a") and sometimes do nothing. Cause: these functions call Claude Sonnet 5.5, which thinks before answering by default, and the thinking counts against the answer's ceiling (`max_tokens`). With ceilings of 300 to 2000 tokens the thinking used most of the room, so the answer was cut off, came back empty, or (stage names, JSON) failed to parse. Every run returned OK in 11 to 16 seconds, so nothing showed in the logs. Fix: `_shared/claude-json.js` has `quickJson` (thinking off the way this model allows, `between_tools`, and a 4000 token ceiling) and `claudeText` (reads the answer and raises "The AI answer came back cut off. Try again." or "The AI declined this one" instead of saving half an answer). Used by `generate-sow`, `generate-scope`, `read-electricity-bill`, `generate-installer-summary`, `generate-invoice-notes`, `generate-lead-summary`, `generate-prebuild-description`, `extract-credential-doc`, `extract-email-job`, `analyze-csv-columns`, `cleanup-text`, `suggest-prebuild-components`, `suggest-stage-prebuilds` and `_shared/word-solar-tasks`. The functions that already handled thinking (polish text, writing style, the chat, email agents) are unchanged. Not test-called before release (no API key reaches the cloud session): the first live click on Q1017 was the test.

## 80. Bill photos too small to read, stock photos on the scope page, cut-off project paragraph (bug, found and fixed 12 Nov 2026, LIVE straight to `main`)

- **Bill reader.** Q1017's three bill pages were 564 by 423 pixels (sent on as "Attachment.jpeg", sideways), so Read bill with AI found no usage. `read-electricity-bill.js` now measures each photo (`_shared/image-size.js`, JPEG, PNG and GIF from their bytes) and turns away any page under 1000 pixels on its long side before the AI call: "Pages 1, 2 and 3 are too small to read (564 by 423 pixels). Delete them and add the retailer's PDF, or new photos taken with the phone camera, one page each." It also keeps the AI's thinking on (effort low, 8000 tokens), undoing item 79's thinking-off for this one function, since reading a photo is where thinking helps.
- **Scope of works page photos.** The two photos came from Settings > portfolio photos copied onto the quote by type ("solar" for a Battery proposal too) and topped up with showcase photos from the code, so a client saw other people's systems with no caption. Owner, 12 Nov 2026: only the client's photos or similar systems. The page now shows the first two Pictures of your system (step 4: their site, this job's pictures, recent installs with this gear) with captions, and Your system shows the rest. No pictures, no photos on that page.
- **The Project page** repeated the scope's opening paragraph, cut off at four lines. Removed.
- Left for the weekly cleanup: `projects.cover_photos` is still written by new quotes (`new-project.html`), duplicates and `create-job-from-quote.js`, and nothing reads it now.

## 81. Ask suppliers for prices from a quote (RFQ), their quotes on a Supplier quotes tab, Approve issues the PO (planned 13 Nov 2026, waiting on yes)

**Owner, 13 Nov 2026:** in quotes, email suppliers asking for prices (like a PO, but not an order: nothing is approved yet), attach the quote the supplier sends back to the job, and approve it to issue the PO. Also a bug: any email sent from a quote's Emails tab marks the quote Sent to Client. **Owner's numbering and approval rules (same day):** the request number is RFQ plus the quote number (RFQ1017 for Q1017; a job with no quote uses its J number), one number shared by every supplier asked on that quote. A Supplier quotes tab lists each quote a supplier sends back with Approve and Deny; Approve issues a PO to that supplier (a new PO number with the RFQ reference on it). Several quotes from one supplier approved on the same day go on one PO; approved on different days, separate POs.

**Part A, the bug (fix into `main`, no migration).** `openJobEmailComposer` (`project.html`) treats every new email from a quote as the quote going out and moves it to Quote Sent on send (the Emails tab's Email the client button, the Send step's button, any supplier email from there). Fix: the quote moves to Quote Sent only when the quote PDF went in that email (the Send step's Email quote to client, or the Quote PDF box ticked in the email window); the email window hands `onSent` the files that went. The same send sets `projects.sent_at` (the client portal reads it; nothing sets it for quotes sent from the app today).

**Part B, the feature (into `staging`).**
1. **Tables.** `supplier_quote_requests` (one per supplier asked: project, supplier, the lines sent as a snapshot, email thread, sent date, who) and `supplier_quotes` (one per quote a supplier sends back: project, supplier, request, the quote as a `project_documents` row, the supplier's quote number and date, quoted total, status quoted / approved / denied, approved date, the PO it went on, who). `supplier_quote_lines` (Stock item, description, quantity, unit, quoted unit cost). The RFQ number is not stored: it is RFQ plus the project's Q number (or J number). Everyone reads; pricing roles write (fast RLS, one policy per action, an index on every FK). One database function `supplier_quote_approve` (pricing roles): approves the quote and makes or extends the PO in one step (the same supplier's PO made by an approval today, Sydney date, on this job takes the lines; otherwise a new PO: lines known, quoted prices, the supplier's quote attached, description "From RFQ1017, their quote 48213 dated 13 Nov"). `supplier_quote_deny` marks it denied.
2. **Supplier quotes tab** (`public/js/supplier-quotes.js`, its own file, loaded on demand) on a quote and on a job, laid out the whole-screen way: the requests on the left (who was asked, when, the lines), the supplier quotes on the right (supplier, their number and date, total, the PDF, Approve and Deny, or the PO it became). **Ask suppliers for prices**: tick one or more suppliers (the Create PO picker), the lines come in from the quote's material lines matched to Stock the way Suggest POs does (all ticked, quantities from the quote; untick, edit, add), Send makes the PDF (`po.html` in request mode: RFQ1017, our details, site, lines and quantities, no prices, no PO number, "a request for pricing, not an order") and opens the email window to each supplier with it attached, tagged to the job as Materials quote (one email per supplier, sent one after the other). A request row says Sent 13 Nov.
3. **The supplier's quote files itself.** When a reply with a file arrives on that conversation (or any email on the job that says RFQ1017 from that supplier's address), the mailbox worker copies it into the job's Documents under Supplier quotes (the same server copy as Save to job, factored into `_shared/`), makes the supplier quote row, reads it with the bill reader (Tally: their quote number, date, lines, prices, total) onto the lines where they match, and rings the bell ("Ideal quoted RFQ1017: $4,120"). A quote that comes another way: Save to job on any email on the job offers "This is a quote for RFQ1017 from ...", and the tab has Attach a supplier's quote (upload, read the same way).
4. **Approve and Deny.** Approve on a supplier quote runs `supplier_quote_approve` and then shows the usual "PO2114 created: Email it to the supplier now" panel (or "added to PO2114" when today's PO for that supplier already exists, with Email it again). The PO page shows "From RFQ1017" with the supplier's quote under its documents. Requests, quotes and POs move to the job with the quote when the client approves (`create-job-from-quote.js`). Suggest POs leaves out lines already ordered from an approved supplier quote.

**Done means:** (1) on a draft quote with material lines: Supplier quotes tab > Ask suppliers for prices > Ideal and Haymans > Send: two emails go with the RFQ1017 PDF, two request rows say Sent, the quote is still a Draft quote; (2) Emails tab > Email the client with the Quote PDF box unticked: still Draft; ticked: Quote Sent; (3) Ideal replies with a PDF: within a few minutes a supplier quote row appears with the total and the PDF is in Documents > Supplier quotes; (4) Approve it: PO2114 is made with the quoted prices and "From RFQ1017", and Email it to the supplier now is offered; approve a second Ideal quote the same day: it joins PO2114; (5) Suggest POs no longer offers those lines; Deny marks a quote denied and makes no PO.

Touches: `public/project.html` (the `onSent` fix; the tab entry and its mount, no other inline code), `public/js/supplier-quotes.js` (new), `public/js/email-compose.js` (`onSent` gets the files that went, `?v=`), `public/js/email-job-files.js` (Save to job offers the request), `public/po.html` (request mode), `public/purchase-order.html` (From RFQ line), `netlify/functions/generate-client-document-pdf.js` (doc type `supplier_quote_request`), `netlify/functions/save-email-attachment.js` and a new `_shared/save-email-attachment.js`, `netlify/functions/_shared/email-agent.js` (the quote-in step in the mailbox worker), `netlify/functions/_shared/create-job-from-quote.js` (move requests and quotes), one migration (`supplier_quote_requests`, `supplier_quotes`, `supplier_quote_lines`, `supplier_quote_approve`, `supplier_quote_deny`), docs. Overlaps: none (open PR 34 is the old email-workers branch; `claude/bill-reading-s905hi` is the bill reader). Kind: Part A bug fix into `main`, no migration; Part B feature into `staging`, migration yes; money no (money enters only through the existing PO path), stock no, RLS yes (new tables: backup before the production migration), payroll no. Size: large (a day). Test on staging: admin@staging.test; the Gmail key on staging for the send and the supplier's reply (or insert the reply by SQL for step 3). Simpler: nobody retypes the materials list for a supplier, files the supplier's quote by hand, or retypes quoted prices onto a PO, and a supplier email from a quote no longer moves it to Quote Sent. Still a tap: Approve or Deny on each supplier quote (the owner's call between suppliers and on timing) and the To address when a supplier has no email saved. Best model: Fable 5.1, effort xhigh, because it is new tables with RLS and a server function plus five pages and a worker in one go.

## 82. Supplier credit notes against POs (planned 13 Nov 2026, waiting on yes)

**Owner, 13 Nov 2026:** "PO2009 has some items accepted that will be credited. We should allow credit notes from suppliers to credit POs."

**Today.** A supplier's credit note emailed to invoice@ is read by Tally (`document_type` credit_note) and then sits in To check on the Emailed bills page saying "This looks like a credit note, not a bill": Save bill would record it as money we owe, so nobody saves it, and nothing nets off. Three are waiting now: IES 20726460 (6 weatherproof battens returned on PO2008, $286.11), Tradezone 561880 ($53.33) and 561191 ($563.90). PO2009 (Tradezone): bill M92798 ($691.67) had three lines not on the PO, Accept put them on it, and Tradezone is crediting them, so the PO waits for goods that will never arrive and the bill sits in the pay run at its full amount. Bill checks already notes "credit note coming" as an Accept as is reason, and the statement reader lists credits but ignores them.

**The idea: a credit note is a bill with a minus in front.** One row in `supplier_bills` with `kind` credit_note and a negative amount, its lines in `supplier_bill_line_items` with negative quantities linked to the PO's lines. Everything that adds up bills (the Suppliers page balances, Pay bills due, the bill check's PO totals, statement reconciliation) nets it off by itself. No new table, no second list, no "apply credit" step.

1. **Saving a credit note** (`supplier_credit_note_save(p jsonb)`, one database step, all or nothing, pricing roles; migration adds `supplier_bills.kind` bill / credit_note, amount below zero only on a credit note):
   - the credit row: their credit number, date, the PO, the file; **approved for payment at once** with pay-on today (nothing to approve, it is their money back), so it joins the next pay run; logged on the supplier, the PO and the job.
   - each line matched to a PO line the same way a bill's lines are (part number, Stock item, description, `matchPoLine`); a line matching nothing is a money-only credit (no stock change), shown as such.
   - what a matched line does to the PO, decided from the price: **Returned** when the credit's unit price is the PO line's price (within 1%), the goods went back. Line not received yet: its quantity drops by the credited quantity (the line goes at zero), so the PO no longer waits for it and can close. Line received: `_po_line_take_back` takes it off the job's cost or out of Stock or the van (refused if the client has already been invoiced for it: the credit still saves, money only, and the message says why), then the quantity drops. **Price credit** when the unit price differs (a discount, a price correction, a freight credit): the goods stay, the PO line's unit cost (and the job's cost rows) drop to what we end up paying. The save card shows the decision per line before saving ("6 x batten returned: comes out of Stock", "price credit on ...: goods kept") with one tap to switch a line, because a credit note does not say whether goods went back and the price is the best tell.
2. **Emailed bills page** (`supplier-bills.html`): a credit note's card reads "Credit note 20726460 from Ideal Electrical: $286.11 against PO2008", the PO picker suggests the PO on the document, else the supplier's PO whose bills carry those part numbers, the lines with what will happen, and **Save credit note** (Already paid hidden; Not a bill and Delete stay). The intake (`_shared/supplier-bill-intake.js`) matches a credit's lines against bills on the supplier's POs, not against the PO's order lines. Tally's prompt: a credit note's quantities and totals as positive numbers (IES came back negative, Tradezone positive; the code takes the size either way).
3. **Uploading a paper one** on the supplier page: the review screen reads `document_type`, its heading says Credit note, and Save runs the same function.
4. **Pay bills due** (`run-supplier-payments.js`, `suppliers.html`): a credit is a minus row in its supplier's batch and the amount to transfer is the net. `supplier_bills_mark_paid` refuses a supplier whose net is below zero ("Credit bigger than the bills for Tradezone: wait for the next bill, or press Refunded on the credit"); a net of zero records both as settled. Supplier page bills table: a Credit chip, the negative amount, and **Refunded** (Admin/Finance, the Paid step with method refund) for when the money comes back to the card or account instead.
5. **PO page** Bills fold (`renderPoBills`): "Credit 20726460 · 9 Nov · -$286.11" with a Credit chip, each line green "6 x $43.35 returned, taken out of Stock" or "price credit"; the bills total on the PO nets.
6. **Bill checks** (`_shared/bill-check.js`, `supplier-checks.html`): credits never appear in To approve; the one problem a credit can have is being bigger than what has been billed on that PO ("Credit $600 on PO2009, bills on it come to $450"). A bill's over-quantity check sums bill lines only; the PO total check nets the credits.
7. **Statements** (`_shared/statement-intake.js`): the credit lines on a statement are matched by number against our credit notes ("Credit 561880 ($53.33) is on the statement but not in TEops", amounts that differ), the same as invoices.
8. **Xero:** supplier bills are not pushed to Xero from TEops (H4 not built), so the credit is entered in Xero by hand as the bills are today.

**PO2009 after this:** Tradezone's credit arrives at invoice@, Save credit note against PO2009: the three accepted lines come off the PO (not received, so nothing moves in Stock), the PO can close, and M92798 and the credit net to nothing in Pay bills due. Bill M94930's ten lines not on the PO are decided as today (Accept or Deny charge).

**Done means:** (1) Emailed bills page: the IES credit note card says Credit note, $286.11 against PO2008, "6 x batten returned: comes out of Stock"; Save credit note; the PO2008 page shows the credit under Bills with the line green and the Warehouse count of that batten down by 6 (or the job's materials down by 6 when the line went to the job). (2) Suppliers page: Ideal's "Invoiced, not paid" is $286.11 lower; Pay bills due lists the credit as a minus under Ideal and the supplier total is the net. (3) A credit bigger than the supplier's bills: Record as paid refuses with the plain message; Refunded on the supplier page records it. (4) A credit note uploaded on a supplier page opens the review screen headed Credit note and saves the same way. (5) Bill checks: no credit in To approve; a credit larger than the PO's bills shows under Problems. (6) `npm run check` green.

Touches: `supabase/migrations/20261202000000_supplier_credit_notes.sql` (new: `supplier_bills.kind`, `supplier_credit_note_save`, `supplier_bills_mark_paid` net rule and refund method), `public/js/supabase-client.js` (`renderPoBills`, `billStatusChip`), `netlify/functions/_shared/supplier-bill-intake.js` (credit branch of `analyse`, `saveInboxItemAsCredit`), `netlify/functions/_shared/supplier-invoice-ai.js` (prompt line), `netlify/functions/_shared/bill-check.js` (credit check, bill-only quantities), `netlify/functions/_shared/statement-intake.js` (credit lines), `netlify/functions/supplier-bill-inbox.js` (save routes by kind), `netlify/functions/run-supplier-payments.js` (credits in the preview, net per supplier, refund), `public/supplier-bills.html` (credit card), `public/supplier-detail.html` (Credit chip, Refunded, review screen credit mode), `public/suppliers.html` (pay run modal), `public/supplier-checks.html` (credits out of To approve), `public/purchase-order.html` (`?v=` bump), `docs/PROJECT_SPEC.md` A11c.
